PARLESPRINT · TCF CANADA
French A2.
Build independence.
Twelve original units for understanding, speaking, reading and writing about familiar life in Canada.
COMPLETE COURSE · REVIEWER DRAFT · 13 OCTOBER 2026

All 12 planned A2 units are included. This copy contains the lesson source, original dialogues and stories, listening and reading tasks, writing and speaking models, answer key, audio scripts and vocabulary index. It is generated from the same authored chapters exported to the app.

Prepared for review by a fluent French speaker and a TCF Canada specialist. Human language, regional-use and audio review is still pending.

Scope This is an A2 learning stage. It is not an official TCF paper, proof of an attained CEFR level, a CLB/NCLC 7 result, or a guarantee of a test outcome.
REVIEW BRIEF
What to assess

The learner is an adult beginner in Canada. A typical study setup is ParleSprint on the left side of an iPad and personal notes on the right. The course uses explicit, concise English grammar support, then asks the learner to listen, read, speak or write in French, record a useful pattern and one error, close the notes, retrieve the language, and use it in a changed situation.

Each unit has original vocabulary and phrases, grammar, two practical dialogues, a connected story, listening and reading questions, a writing task, speaking practice, a TCF-style corner and a cumulative checkpoint. Most units add 48 new vocabulary entries; Units 1 and 12 add 45 each, for 570 planned new entries across A2.

Review the language and teaching

  • Check French grammar, idiom, agreement, register and the English meaning of every vocabulary example, dialogue, story and model.
  • Check that A2 language remains understandable for a learner moving up from A1, and that no explanation hides a required step.
  • Check the order and completeness of the grammar sequence, especially the transition from completed past actions to background and event narration.
  • Check Canadian context and label regional expressions clearly. In particular, review rental, transit, health-service, delivery and grocery wording for Ontario/Canadian use.
  • Check every answer key against the supporting text or recording script; note ambiguous distractors and items with more than one plausible answer.
  • Review the sound guides as rough English-language aids. They are not IPA and need a French speaker's correction; listen to the app voice separately.
  • Judge whether each writing/speaking prompt is achievable at this point in the course and whether the model is a useful example rather than a script to memorize.

For comments, identify the unit, section and item ID; quote the words at issue; suggest a correction; and say whether it is a French-language, level, fact, answer-key or teaching issue.

Course time is a workload estimate

The plan allocates 14-16 focused hours per unit, or 168-192 hours for A2. That is an estimate for study, delayed review and independent transfer, not a calendar rule or a research-validated guarantee. At two hours a day, it is roughly 12-14 weeks if studied every day, or 17-20 weeks at five days a week. At four hours a day, it is about 8-10 weeks at five days a week. The learner should move forward based on delayed recall and unfamiliar-context performance, not screen completion alone.

The review cycle starts with suggested retrieval gaps of 1, 3, 7, 14 and 30 days, moving an item sooner when recall fails. The design combines adult-friendly explicit explanation with contextual French and repeated retrieval; it does not claim to reproduce how infants acquire language or that AI makes learning faster. Second-language meta-analyses support spaced practice on average and report positive average outcomes for generative AI chatbots, with variation across studies. Neither finding proves that this specific course accelerates French learning.

Research references: Kim and Webb (2022), Language Learning, DOI 10.1111/lang.12479; Li, Wang and Yang (2025), Journal of Computer Assisted Learning, DOI 10.1111/jcal.70060. CEFR reference: Council of Europe, CEFR Companion Volume.

EXAM REFERENCE
What the official TCF Canada contains

The TCF Canada has four required components. The figures below follow the official France Éducation international overview and candidate information; recheck the current source before release.

ComponentOfficial formatTime
Listening39 multiple-choice questions; each recording is heard once35 minutes
Reading39 multiple-choice questions60 minutes
Writing3 tasks. Task 1: 60-120 words; Task 2: 120-150; Task 3: 120-18060 minutes
Speaking3 tasks with an examiner12 minutes

A2 practice in this manuscript is original and progressively scaffolded. It practices short listening and reading items and the full Task 1 writing range; later writing and speaking task shapes appear as supported previews where stated. This is not a complete official-length test pack and does not claim that completing A2 is enough for a TCF or CLB/NCLC 7 result.

Official sources: France Éducation international: TCF Canada and official sample tasks.

In Canada The course uses fictional service, rental, transit and shopping examples. They teach language for asking, comparing and explaining; they do not give medical, tenancy, legal, billing or current-service advice.
A2 COURSE MAP
Twelve units, a deliberate progression

New grammar appears in context, then returns in later units and in the capstone. The word target is a planning target, not an official CEFR vocabulary count.

Unit and new wordsCan-do goalNew grammar
01
45 new items		A week in my life: connect the details
Connect several short present-tense ideas, give a simple reason or contrast, ask someone to clarify, and write a short connected message.		Connect clauses and organize a short answer: et, mais, parce que, donc, d'abord, ensuite, enfin
02
48 new items		What happened this week?
Describe completed everyday actions in the recent past and understand their order in a short message.		Passé composé with avoir and regular -er past participles
03
48 new items		Everyday events: what I did
Describe completed familiar actions with several common irregular past forms and place events in a clear order.		Common irregular past forms with avoir and a starter set of common verbs with être
04
48 new items		Tell a clear story
Use past-event forms to make a simple story understandable, with a background, event and outcome.		Imparfait for past habits, descriptions and background; Choose passé composé for completed events and imparfait for background or habit
05
48 new items		Work, study and future plans
Talk about near plans and simple future intentions, and understand dates and conditions in a schedule.		Futur proche review and common futur simple forms for plans and predictions
06
48 new items		Choose a home and describe a neighbourhood
Compare two familiar places and combine information into clearer descriptions.		Comparatives and superlatives for familiar choices
07
48 new items		Health, appointments and getting help
Explain a simple health or service problem and refer to a thing already mentioned.		Relative pronouns qui, que and où to combine familiar details
08
48 new items		Ask, answer and follow up at work or school
Ask who receives an action, give someone information, and keep a short exchange going.		Direct object pronouns le, la and les before the verb
09
48 new items		Travel across the city and beyond
Refer to places without repeating every place name and describe a simple trip or change.		Indirect object pronouns lui and leur with common verbs; Y for a previously named place with common aller/être patterns
10
48 new items		Food, shopping and quantities
Talk about quantities without repeating the whole noun and compare familiar choices.		En for quantities with common partitive and quantity patterns
11
48 new items		Solve a practical problem politely
Ask someone to take a practical action politely, give a simple instruction and explain what needs to change.		Affirmative and negative imperative for simple instructions; Polite conditional requests with pouvoir and vouloir; build on je voudrais
12
45 new items		A2 capstone: handle a changing week
Understand the main point and relevant details in short connected texts, manage a routine interaction, tell a simple story and write a clear practical message.		Cumulative retrieval and transfer

A2 · UNIT 1
A2.1
A week in my life: connect the details
AFTER THIS UNIT YOU CAN
Describe a familiar week in connected sentences, give a simple reason or contrast, ask for clarification, and write a short practical message.
THE SITUATION
An adult learner balances work, French study and daily responsibilities in Canada. The goal is to explain a real week, understand another person's schedule and keep the exchange going.
In Canada Use the schedule examples as language practice, not as current transit, clinic or workplace information. Real opening hours and service details change.
In Canada This unit is designed for split-screen study: make one short note in your notes app, close it, retrieve the pattern aloud, then reuse it with different details.

1.1 Words: habits and the shape of a week

Picture it: on Sunday evening, Awa checks her work and French-class schedule. One plan has changed, and she needs to explain why to a classmate. You already know days, times and basic present-tense verbs. Add the words that help you explain a week; before checking the English, listen and guess each from its example.

In Canada Use the same clear standard French across Canada. Work schedules, class schedules and service hours vary by location; the examples here are invented for practice.

1.2 Phrases: explain a real week

Try each phrase before reading its meaning. Choose one that is true for you, then change the day, time or activity so it describes your own week.

1.3 Grammar: connect ideas and explain why (g23)

At A1, you learned to make clear short sentences. At A2, keep the same clear word order and connect two ideas so the listener can follow your schedule, reason or choice. A connector joins ideas; it does not change the verb form.

A. add, contrast and give a reason

Choose the relationship between the ideas
ConnectorWhat it doesExample
etadds informationJe travaille le matin et j'étudie le soir.
maisshows a contrastJe suis occupé lundi, mais je suis libre mardi.
parce quegives a reasonJe reporte le cours parce que je travaille.
doncshows a resultLe bus est en retard, donc j'arrive plus tard.
  • Say the two ideas first. Then choose the connector that shows their relationship: addition, contrast, reason or result.
  • After parce que, use a subject and a verb: parce que je travaille.
  • Use donc for the result: Je travaille, donc je reporte le cours.
  • Avoid joining every sentence with et. A precise connector helps another person understand your point.

B. put events in an easy order

A small sequence makes a longer answer easier to follow
OrderExample
firstD'abord, je vérifie mon horaire.
nextEnsuite, je planifie mes tâches.
lastEnfin, je prépare mon sac.

Notebook move: write one true sentence about your week. Close your notes and say it again with a different connector. Tomorrow, retrieve the idea first, then rebuild the French without looking.

READING 1.3
Choose the connector
Choose the connector that matches the relationship between the ideas.
1. Je travaille le matin ___ j'étudie le soir. (addition)
A parce queB etC doncD mais
2. Je suis occupée lundi, ___ je suis libre mardi. (contrast)
A maisB doncC parce queD d'abord
3. Je reporte le cours ___ je travaille. (reason)
A ensuiteB maisC parce queD donc
4. Le bus est en retard, ___ j'arrive plus tard. (result)
A d'abordB parce queC maisD donc

1.4 Dialogues: explain, check, repair

Listen for the main schedule first. Then notice how the speakers ask a follow-up question and check a detail instead of pretending they understood.

A schedule that changed
Nadia and Sam compare their work and French-study weeks.
NadiaD'habitude, je révise le mardi soir, mais cette semaine j'ai un atelier.I usually review on Tuesday evening, but this week I have a workshop.
SamTu as un atelier au travail ?Do you have a workshop at work?
NadiaNon, un atelier de français. Il commence à dix-huit heures.No, a French workshop. It starts at six p.m.
SamSi j'ai bien compris, tu ne peux pas réviser mardi.If I understood correctly, you can't review Tuesday.
NadiaOui, c'est ça. Je vais réviser mercredi parce que je suis libre.Yes, that's right. I am going to review Wednesday because I am free.
SamEt tu prends une pause quand ?And when do you take a break?
NadiaJe prends une pause après le travail, puis je me repose le soir.I take a break after work, then I rest in the evening.
Clarify the meeting time
A learner checks a class schedule with a classmate.
SamOn planifie notre séance de français. Tu es libre jeudi ?We are planning our French session. Are you free Thursday?
JulieOui, mais je termine le travail à dix-sept heures.Yes, but I finish work at five p.m.
SamJe veux dire jeudi à dix-huit heures.I mean Thursday at six p.m.
JulieSi j'ai bien compris, la séance commence à dix-huit heures ?If I understood correctly, the session starts at six p.m.?
SamOui. D'abord, on révise le français; ensuite, on parle de notre semaine.Yes. First, we review French; then we talk about our week.
JulieD'accord. Je prends des notes et je prépare une question.Okay. I will take notes and prepare a question.
READING 1.4
Follow the schedules
Choose the best answer from the dialogues.
1. Why does Nadia change her usual Tuesday review?
A She has a French workshop.B She is travelling.C She has a clinic appointment.D She is working all night.
2. When will Nadia review instead?
A MondayB WednesdayC ThursdayD Friday
3. What time does Julie finish work on Thursday?
A At 5 p.m.B At 6 p.m.C At 7 p.m.D At noon
4. What will Sam and Julie do first?
A Prepare a mealB Read a noticeC Review FrenchD Call the teacher

1.5 Story: a week with one change

STORY
Une semaine bien organisée
A well-organized week
Chaque dimanche, Awa organise sa semaine.
Every Sunday, Awa organizes her week.
D'habitude, elle travaille le matin et elle étudie le soir.
Usually, she works in the morning and studies in the evening.
Cette semaine, elle a une présentation au travail.
This week, she has a presentation at work.
Elle prépare une liste de tâches. Sa présentation est une priorité.
She prepares a to-do list. Her presentation is a priority.
Mardi, un imprévu change son horaire.
On Tuesday, an unexpected event changes her schedule.
Elle reporte sa séance de français à mercredi.
She postpones her French session until Wednesday.
Awa prend une pause, donc elle peut se concentrer ensuite.
Awa takes a break, so she can concentrate afterwards.
Le soir, elle révise ses notes et fait des progrès, mais elle prend aussi une pause pour se reposer.
In the evening, she reviews her notes and makes progress, but she also takes a break to rest.
READING 1.5
Understand the story
Answer from the story. Find the clue before you choose.
1. When does Awa usually organize her week?
A Every SundayB Every TuesdayC Every morningD Every Friday
2. What changes Awa's schedule?
A A French examB An unexpected eventC A new jobD A family visit
3. When is the French session now?
A MondayB TuesdayC WednesdayD Thursday
4. What does Awa do after rereading her notes?
A She sees progress.B She goes to work.C She cancels the workshop.D She changes cities.

1.6 Listening: hear the main point and one detail

On the first play, identify the general schedule or change. On the next item, listen for one specific detail such as a day, reason or result. Do not try to translate every word.

1.7 Reading: scan a message and a schedule

Look first for the purpose: is the person explaining a routine, a change or a question? Then scan for the day, time and reason. These are original practice texts, not messages from a real service.

1.8 Writing: send a connected weekly message

WRITING 1.8W
Write to a classmate
TCF Canada writing Task 1 asks for a short message in the 60-120 word range. Write 60-80 French words to a classmate: describe two parts of your usual week, explain one change this week, give a reason, and suggest a time to meet. Use a greeting and a clear closing. Draft before reading the model.
Check your text:
  • Write 60-80 words for this practice; the official Task 1 range is 60-120 words.
  • Include the usual routine, the change and its reason.
  • Use at least two connectors accurately.
  • Check that each sentence has a subject and a verb.
  • Close your notes and rewrite the message tomorrow from the key ideas only.

1.9 Speaking: explain your week, then adapt

SPEAKING 1.9S
Answer follow-up questions
First answer without notes for 30-45 seconds: what does a usual week look like, what is one priority, and when do you study or rest? Add one reason or contrast. Then ask Marie for one follow-up question. If needed, request one hint, try again, and ask Marie to change one day or responsibility so you must adapt the answer.
  • Answer the question asked; do not recite the model.
  • Use one connector to explain a reason and one to show a contrast or result.
  • If you do not understand, ask for repetition or clarification in French.
  • After one correction, repeat the answer with a changed day or activity.

1.10 TCF corner and checkpoint

TCF Canada includes separate listening, reading, speaking and writing tests. This unit uses original short practice tasks; it does not predict a score. For Task 1 writing, count your words and keep the message within 60-120 words.

LISTENING 1.10L
TCF listening practice

A Thursday after 15:00B Wednesday morningC Friday at noonD Sunday evening
2. What does the speaker do second?
A Checks the deadlineB Finishes the presentationC Takes a breakD Changes the schedule
3. Why is the class postponed?
A The speaker has an unexpected work issue.B The teacher is sick.C The room is closed.D The speaker forgot the time.

2. What should the learner do after each session?

Checkpoint: 10 questions
Aim for 8/10, then explain one answer aloud without looking at the key.
1. What does une habitude mean?
A A habitB A deadlineC A workshopD A presentation
2. Which phrase means 'generally'?
A en retardB en généralC à tempsD en forme

A A breakB A sessionC A deadlineD A goal
4. Choose the connector for a reason: Je reporte le cours ___ je travaille.
A doncB maisC parce queD ensuite
5. Choose the connector for a result: Il est tard, ___ je termine demain.
A doncB parce queC d'abordD mais
6. What does se reposer mean?
A To organizeB To restC To clarifyD To postpone
7. What does un imprévu mean?
A An unexpected eventB A weekly goalC A colleagueD A note
8. What does préciser mean?
A To postponeB To clarify or specifyC To wake upD To meet a deadline
9. Which sequence is in the correct order?
A Enfin, d'abord, ensuiteB Ensuite, enfin, d'abordC D'abord, ensuite, enfinD Parce que, mais, donc
10. A class is postponed because of a work problem. Which sentence states the reason clearly?
A Je reporte le cours parce que j'ai un imprévu au travail.B Je reporte le cours, donc j'ai un imprévu au travail.C Je reporte le cours, mais j'ai un imprévu au travail.D Je reporte le cours ensuite parce que je suis libre demain.

Unit 1: check your progress

  • I can explain a usual week and one change with connected sentences.
  • I can use et, mais, parce que and donc to show how ideas relate.
  • I can put simple actions in order with d'abord, ensuite and enfin.
  • I can ask someone to clarify a time or detail and confirm what I understood.
  • I can write a short TCF Task 1 style message and check its length.
  • I can try a new weekly schedule without copying the dialogue or model.

2.3 Grammar: completed actions with regular -er verbs (g24)

For the verbs practised in this lesson, build the passé composé with the present of avoir plus a past participle. Most French verbs use avoir, but some common verbs use être; Unit 3 introduces a starter set. For a regular -er verb such as réserver, remove -er and add -é. The subject still controls the form of avoir.

A finished action: avoir + a regular past participle
SubjectAvoirExample
j'aiJ'ai réservé une place.
tuasTu as téléphoné hier.
il / elle / onaElle a terminé son devoir.
nousavonsNous avons étudié ensemble.
vousavezVous avez confirmé la visite.
ils / ellesontIls ont préparé le projet.
  • Look for the time clue, then ask: what finished action happened?
  • Keep avoir in the present: j'ai, tu as, elle a. Put the main action in the past participle.
  • Most regular -er participles end in -é. Hear the difference between je réserve (now) and j'ai réservé (finished).
  • Do not add -s to the past participle with avoir just because the subject is plural: elles ont réservé.
READING 2.3
Build the completed action
Choose the form that completes each sentence.
1. Hier, nous ___ la salle.
A avons réservéB sommes réserverC avons réserverD réservons hier
2. Elle ___ son devoir hier soir.
A a terminéB est terminerC a terminerD termine hier
3. Vous ___ la visite ?
A avez confirméB avez confirmerC êtes confirméD confirmé avez
4. Which sentence describes a finished action?
A Ils ont étudié hier.B Ils étudient demain.C Ils vont étudier demain.D Ils étudient maintenant.

2.4 Dialogues: explain what happened

Notice how each speaker gives a time, an action and one useful detail. The listener asks a short follow-up question.

The parcel came late
Awa asks a neighbour whether a delivery notice arrived.
AwaTu as trouvé un avis de passage hier ?Did you find a delivery notice yesterday?
SamOui. J'ai trouvé l'avis près de la porte hier soir.Yes. I found the notice by the door yesterday evening.
AwaMerci. Le colis est pour moi; je téléphone au service aujourd'hui.Thanks. The parcel is for me; I am calling the service today.
SamMalheureusement, la livraison a commencé en retard.Unfortunately, the delivery started late.
AwaJ'ai appelé le service, puis j'ai réclamé un remboursement.I called the service, then requested a refund.
SamEt quel événement a changé ton horaire ?And what event changed your schedule?
AwaHeureusement, ils ont confirmé la livraison pour demain.Fortunately, they confirmed the delivery for tomorrow.
After the course
Two classmates compare their afternoons.
NadiaTu as étudié après le cours ?Did you study after class?
SamJ'ai emprunté un livre et j'ai préparé mon exposé.I borrowed a book and prepared my presentation.
NadiaTu as terminé le devoir aussi ?Did you finish the assignment too?
SamOui, j'ai terminé le devoir à six heures.Yes, I finished the assignment at six.
NadiaMoi, j'ai marché vingt minutes après le cours.I walked for twenty minutes after class.
SamTu as commencé le cours à l'heure prévue ?Did you start class at the scheduled time?
NadiaOui, finalement. J'ai marché jusqu'à la station.Yes, in the end. I walked to the station.

2.5 Story: a day with three finished tasks

STORY
Une journée bien remplie
A busy day
Mardi, Awa a commencé sa journée tôt.
On Tuesday, Awa started her day early.
Elle a terminé un devoir avant son cours.
She finished an assignment before her class.
Après le cours, elle a réservé une visite guidée.
After class, she booked a guided tour.
Ensuite, elle a emprunté un livre à la bibliothèque.
Then she borrowed a book from the library.
Plus tard, elle a trouvé un avis de passage.
Later, she found a delivery notice.
Elle a appelé le service au sujet du retard, puis elle a réclamé un remboursement.
She called the service to report the delay, then requested a refund.
Heureusement, le service a confirmé la livraison pour mercredi.
Fortunately, the service confirmed the delivery for Wednesday.
Le soir, Awa a préparé ses notes et a téléphoné à une amie.
In the evening, Awa prepared her notes and called a friend.

2.8 Writing: recount a recent activity

WRITING 2.8W
Write a short message about yesterday
Write 60-80 French words to a classmate. Say what you did yesterday, put at least three finished actions in order, and add one reaction or reason. The official TCF Canada Task 1 range is 60-120 words. Use a greeting and closing.
Check your text:
  • Count the words; practise 60-80, within the official Task 1 range of 60-120.
  • Use present avoir + a past participle for each completed action.
  • Put the events in a clear order.
  • Check the time phrase and the subject of each sentence.
  • Tomorrow, close your notes and retell the same day with different details.

2.9 Speaking: tell Marie what happened

SPEAKING 2.9S
Recount a real or invented day
Speak for 30-45 seconds without notes. Name three things you did yesterday or last week and say when they happened. Add one reason or reaction. Ask Marie one follow-up question, then repeat the account with a different event and time.
  • Use a time expression before or after the action.
  • Build the past form with avoir + the participle; regular -er participles end in -é.
  • If Marie corrects one form, repeat the full sentence once.
  • Change a time or event so the second attempt is not memorized.

A2.3
Everyday events: what I did
AFTER THIS UNIT YOU CAN
Recount familiar completed actions with common irregular forms using avoir and a starter set of common verbs using être; put events in order and answer a follow-up question.
THE SITUATION

The transport and service scenes are invented for practice; station names, routes and opening hours are not current travel information. Standard French is used, with familiar Canadian places only as context.

3.3 Grammar: passé composé: common irregular forms and selected être verbs (g25)

Unit 2 introduced the passé composé with avoir and regular -er participles. This unit adds five common irregular forms used with avoir and a starter set of common verbs used with être. Learn the whole verb pattern, not just the participle.

A. Five high-value irregular forms with avoir

Passé composé: present avoir + past participle
InfinitivePast participleExample
fairefaitJ'ai fait une demande.
prendreprisNous avons pris le train.
voirvuElle a vu le numéro.
lireluVous avez lu le message.
avoireuJ'ai eu une réponse.
  • Learn the participle as part of a pair: prendre → pris, then retrieve j'ai pris from memory.
  • The subject changes avoir (j'ai, tu as, elle a, nous avons); keep the participle form shown in these examples.
  • Put a time or order word beside the action so the listener knows when it happened.
  • Do not guess the irregular form from the infinitive: voir becomes vu, not voi or voyé.
READING 3.3A
Use the pattern
Choose the form that matches the meaning.
1. Hier, j'___ le message deux fois.
A ai luB ai lireC suis luD lis hier
2. Nous ___ le train à huit heures.
A avons prisB avons prendreC sommes prisD prenons hier
3. Elle ___ un détail sur le billet.
A a vuB a voirC est vuD voit hier
4. Tu ___ une demande au guichet.
A as faitB as faireC es faitD fais hier

B. A starter set of common verbs with être

A few common completed actions with être
InfinitivePast participleExample
allerallé(e)(s)Sam est allé au cours. / Lina est allée au cours.
arriverarrivé(e)(s)Le bus est arrivé à l'heure.
partirparti(e)(s)Nadia et Awa sont parties à huit heures.
venirvenu(e)(s)Awa est venue au centre.
rentrerrentré(e)(s)Karim est rentré chez lui.
  • These common intransitive verbs use être in the passé composé: aller, arriver, partir, venir, and rentrer. Learn the auxiliary with each verb.
  • In writing, the past participle agrees with the subject in these examples: il est arrivé; elle est arrivée; ils sont arrivés; elles sont arrivées.
  • Compare the two patterns: Sam est arrivé au centre; Sam a lu le message. The verb tells you which auxiliary to use.
  • This is a starter set of common verbs, not a rule that every verb about movement takes être. Check the auxiliary when you learn a new past-tense verb.
READING 3.3B
Use the pattern
Choose the form that matches the meaning.
1. Hier, Lina ___ au centre.
A est arrivéeB a arrivéC est arriverD arrive hier
2. Nadia et Awa sont ___ à huit heures.
A partiesB partiC partieD partir
3. Which sentence uses the correct auxiliary?
A Sam est arrivé à l'heure.B Sam a arrivé à l'heure.C Sam est arriver à l'heure.D Sam arrive hier à l'heure.

3.4 Dialogues: ask what happened and give the useful detail

Listen for the situation first. Then notice the exact action and the follow-up question; the second speaker checks a detail instead of changing the topic.

A new bus connection
Lina asks Sam how he got to a new French workshop in Ottawa.
LinaTu as pris le bus jusqu'au centre ?Did you take the bus downtown?
SamOui, puis j'ai pris une correspondance près de la gare.Yes, then I took a connection near the train station.
LinaTu as vu le numéro de la ligne sur le billet ?Did you see the route number on the ticket?
SamOui, je l'ai vu à l'accueil.Yes, I saw it at the welcome desk.
LinaEt tu as trouvé la salle sans problème ?And did you find the room without a problem?
SamOui. J'ai lu le plan de la ville et j'ai vu le nom de la salle.Yes. I read the city map and saw the room's name.
LinaTu as eu le temps de lire la consigne ?Did you have time to read the instruction?
SamOui, et j'ai eu le temps de lire la consigne.Yes, and I had time to read the instruction.
After the library visit
Nadia asks Awa about her first visit to a public library.
NadiaTu as lu un article pendant ta première visite à la bibliothèque municipale ?Did you read an article during your first visit to the public library?
AwaOui. J'ai lu un article et j'ai trouvé une séance de français.Yes. I read an article and found a French session.
NadiaTu as pris un livre aussi ?Did you take a book too?
AwaOui, j'ai pris un chapitre facile pour commencer.Yes, I took an easy chapter to start.
NadiaEt tu as eu une carte ?And did you get a card?
AwaPas encore. J'ai fait une demande et j'ai pris le reçu.Not yet. I made an application and took the receipt.
NadiaTu as eu une réponse ?Did you get an answer?
AwaOui, j'ai eu une réponse par courriel ce matin.Yes, I got an answer by email this morning.
READING 3.4
Follow the exchange
Choose the detail each speaker gives.
1. Which connection did Sam take?
A One near the train stationB One at the libraryC One beside the clinicD One at the airport
2. What did Sam read to find the room?
A A city mapB A newspaperC A receiptD A voice message
3. What did Awa do at the public library?
A She read an article and saw a French session.B She bought a train ticket.C She applied for a job.D She met the instructor at a clinic.
4. What did Awa keep after her application?
A The receiptB The city mapC The newspaperD The ticket

3.5 Story: a first day with a new route

Follow one learner through a new trip and first class. On the first read, put the main actions in order; on the second, retell them without looking.

A first class in Ottawa

Sam took the bus downtown.
À la station, il a vu un avis avec le nouvel itinéraire.

À la fin, il a lu un message sur la prochaine séance de français.
At the end, he read a message about the next French session.

3.8 Writing: recount one first experience

WRITING 3.8W
Write a message about a first experience
Write 60-90 French words to a classmate about a first experience in Canada. Say where you went, what you did, one thing you saw or read, and how you felt. Use at least two time-order words. The official TCF Canada writing Task 1 range is 60-120 words. Draft from key ideas before opening the model.
Check your text:
  • Check that the message answers where, what and how you felt.
  • Use at least two time-order phrases and keep actions in a clear order.
  • Check each irregular pair: faire/fait, prendre/pris, voir/vu, lire/lu, avoir/eu.
  • Count 60-90 words for this practice, within the official Task 1 range of 60-120.
  • Close your notes tomorrow and recount a different first experience.

3.9 Speaking: give an unscripted account and handle a follow-up

SPEAKING 3.9S
Tell Marie about a first experience
Speak for 35-50 seconds about a first experience: a new route, class, appointment or activity. Say what you did, what you saw or read and how it ended. Ask Marie one related follow-up question. After her one focused correction, retell the account with a different place or activity.
  • Use a few short connected sentences instead of translating every thought.
  • If you forget a form, describe the action another way and keep communicating.
  • Ask one genuine question, listen to Marie's response, and answer the follow-up in your own words.
  • Use a changed prompt for your second attempt so you retrieve the language instead of reciting.

A2 Unit 3: retrieve, then transfer

  • After one day, close your notes and retrieve five useful words and the grammar pattern.
  • After three and seven days, use the same language in a changed scene; bring back items that still need effort.
  • Record one self-correction and one original sentence in your notes, then say the sentence without reading.
  • Move on when you can do the transfer task with the notes closed; lesson completion alone is not mastery.
A2 · UNIT 4
A2.4
Tell a clear story
AFTER THIS UNIT YOU CAN
Describe a past routine or scene, then report a completed event and its outcome so a short story is easy to follow.
THE SITUATION
A learner describes an ordinary past routine and adds one event that changed what happened next.
In Canada The past routines are personal examples, not claims about all Canadian cities. Housing terms such as sous-sol and cour intérieure depend on the property; confirm details in a real rental listing.

Soft light came through the window.

The past routines are personal examples, not claims about all Canadian cities. Housing terms such as sous-sol and cour intérieure depend on the property; confirm details in a real rental listing.

4.2 Phrases: set a scene before telling the next part

Say the time phrase and the background detail together. Write one true memory in your notes, close them, then describe another place or routine without reading.

Useful connected phrases
FrenchSay itEnglishIn a sentence
Quand j'étais étudiant, je prenais le bus chaque matin.kah(n) zhay-TEH ay-tü-DYAH(N) zhuh pruh-NEH luh büs shahk mah-TA(N)When I was a student, I used to take the bus every morning.
À cette époque, nous habitions près du centre.ah seht ay-POHK noo zah-bee-TYOH(N) preh dü sah(n)trAt that time, we lived near the centre.

4.3 Grammar: set the scene, then report what happened (g26 + g27)

A clear past account often needs two jobs: describe the background or a repeated habit, then report a completed event. Use the imparfait for the first job and the passé composé forms already learned in Units 2 and 3 for the second.

A. Build the imparfait for past habits and descriptions

Use the nous present stem without -ons, then add -ais, -ais, -ait, -ions, -iez, -aient. Être uses ét-.
Subject or verbImparfait formMeaning
parler: jeparlaisI used to speak
parler: nousparlionswe used to speak
habiter: j' / ellehabitais / habitaitI / she used to live
habiter: noushabitionswe used to live
prendre: je / elle / nousprenais / prenait / prenionsI / she / we used to take
étudier: tuétudiaisyou used to study
rentrer: je / nousrentrais / rentrionsI / we used to go home
aller: tuallaisyou used to go
se rendre: tute rendaisyou used to go
se retrouver: je / elleme retrouvais / se retrouvaitI / she used to meet up
lire: je / ellelisais / lisaitI / she used to read
écrire: je / elleécrivais / écrivaitI / she used to write
ouvrir: le commerce / les commercesouvrait / ouvraientthe shop(s) used to open
fermer: il / ilsfermait / fermaientit / they used to close
rester: la piècerestaitthe room stayed
préparer: ma tantepréparaitmy aunt used to prepare
déjeuner: jedéjeunaisI used to have lunch
jouer: les enfantsjouaientthe children used to play
se promener: jeme promenaisI used to walk
réviser: jerévisaisI used to review
être: je / tu / elleétais / étaitI / you / she was
être: nous / ilsétions / étaientwe / they were
avoir: elle / il yavaitshe had; there was
  • Most imparfait forms start from the present nous form: nous parlons → parl-, then add the ending.
  • Être is the main exception: its stem is ét- (j'étais, nous étions).
  • Use it for a repeated past habit (chaque matin, je prenais le bus) or a description (la salle était calme).
  • The endings -ais, -ait, and -aient usually sound alike; use the subject to choose the spelling.
  • In your notes, write the stem and ending separately, then close the notes and rebuild one sentence from memory.
READING 4.3A
Use the pattern
Choose the form that matches the meaning.
1. Quand j'étais jeune, nous ___ près du centre.
A habitionsB habitonsC avons habitéD habiter
2. Chaque hiver, il ___ froid le matin.
A faisaitB faitC a faitD faire
3. Choose the imparfait form of avoir with elle.
A avaitB aC a euD avoir
4. Complete: La pièce ___ une grande fenêtre.
A avaitB aC a euD avoir

B. Choose the tense by the job the idea does

The left column gives a setting, description or usual action. The right column presents one finished event.
Imparfait: background or repeated habitPassé composé: completed event
Le dimanche, nous préparions le repas.Un dimanche, nous avons préparé le repas ensemble.
La salle était calme.Le cours a commencé à neuf heures.
Il y avait une grande fenêtre.J'ai lu le message avant le cours.
Je prenais le bus chaque matin.Un matin, j'ai pris un autre bus.
Ma tante habitait près du collège.Elle a visité le collège samedi.
  • Use the imparfait for a past setting, description, feeling or repeated habit: la salle était calme; chaque matin, je prenais le bus.
  • Use the passé composé to report a completed event: le cours a commencé; j'ai lu le message.
  • The same verb can take either form. Ask whether you are describing what was usual or telling what happened on one occasion.
  • Connect the two ideas with a time link: Je lisais quand le cours a commencé. The first action was in progress; the second is the event.
  • Do not choose only from a time word. Un dimanche can introduce a repeated routine or one particular Sunday; the meaning decides.
  • Draw one long background line and mark a finished event on it. Say the pair once with your notes, then once without them.
READING 4.3B
Use the pattern
Choose the form that matches the meaning.
1. Every Sunday was the family's routine: Le dimanche, nous ___ le repas.
A préparionsB avons préparéC préparonsD préparer
2. One particular Sunday: Nous ___ le repas ensemble.
A avons préparéB préparions chaque dimancheC préparonsD préparer
3. Choose the natural background + event pair.
A La salle était calme quand le cours a commencé.B La salle est calme quand le cours a commencé hier.C La salle était calme quand le cours commencer.D La salle a été calme chaque matin quand le cours commence.
4. What does Je prenais le bus chaque matin tell you?
A A repeated past habitB One completed trip yesterdayC A future planD A polite request

4.4 Dialogues: reminisce about an old routine

Listen for a repeated habit and a description. The speakers are describing what was usual, not listing a series of finished events.

A familiar route
Two classmates talk about their old neighbourhoods.
AwaQuand j'habitais à Gatineau, je prenais le bus chaque matin.When I lived in Gatineau, I used to take the bus every morning.
SamLe trajet était long ?Was the trip long?
AwaOui, mais le quartier était paisible et le bus était silencieux.Yes, but the neighbourhood was peaceful and the bus was quiet.
SamTu prenais ce bus chaque matin ?Did you take this bus every morning?
AwaOui. Il y avait un petit café près de l'arrêt.Yes. There was a small café near the stop.
SamTu y allais après le travail ?Did you go there after work?
AwaParfois. Je retrouvais une amie et nous parlions de nos cours.Sometimes. I met up with a friend and we talked about our classes.
The old study room
Nadia describes the room where she used to study.
NadiaLa salle de lecture était au sous-sol.The reading room was in the basement.
JulieElle était calme ?Was it quiet?
NadiaOui, mais il y avait parfois du bruit dans le couloir.Yes, but sometimes there was noise in the hallway.
JulieTu étudiais là chaque soir ?Did you study there every evening?
NadiaNon, je révisais le matin. La lumière était douce près de la fenêtre.No, I used to review in the morning. The light was soft near the window.
JulieEt tu rentrais à quelle heure ?And what time did you go home?
NadiaJe rentrais à midi et je déjeunais avec ma sœur.I used to go home at noon and have lunch with my sister.
READING 4.4
Follow the exchange
Choose the detail each speaker gives.
1. How did Awa get to work or class in Gatineau?
A She took the bus each morning.B She walked every evening.C She drove downtown.D She took a train at noon.
2. What was near the stop?
A A small caféB A libraryC A playgroundD A train station
3. Where was Nadia's reading room?
A In the basementB On a balconyC Near the stationD In a courtyard
4. Why did Nadia review in the morning?
A The light near the window was soft.B The hallway was busier.C The bus was more frequent.D Her sister worked there.

4.8 Writing: write a brief story with a background and one event

WRITING 4.8W
Tell a classmate what happened
Write 60-90 French words to a classmate. Describe a past routine or place, then tell one completed event that happened during that period and its result. Use at least two imparfait forms, one passé composé form and a time link. This is a short learning task, not a complete TCF writing task.
Check your text:
  • Give the reader a setting or repeated habit before the event.
  • Use the imparfait for the background and passé composé for the completed event.
  • Include a clear time link and explain what happened next.
  • Count 60-90 words and check that every subject matches its verb.
  • Close your notes tomorrow and tell a different short story.

4.9 Speaking: tell a short story with a clear sequence

SPEAKING 4.9S
Tell Marie about a day that changed
Speak for 35-50 seconds about an ordinary past routine and one event that changed a particular day. Set the scene, say what happened, and explain the result. Marie asks one follow-up. After feedback, retell a different event without notes.
  • Start with a scene or repeated routine using the imparfait.
  • Use passé composé to report one finished event; keep the action sequence clear.
  • Say what changed or happened as a result.
  • On the retry, change the place, event and outcome rather than repeating the model.

A2 Unit 4: retrieve, then transfer

  • After one day, close your notes and retrieve five useful words and the grammar pattern.
  • After three and seven days, use the same language in a changed scene; bring back items that still need effort.
  • Record one self-correction and one original sentence in your notes, then say the sentence without reading.
  • Move on when you can do the transfer task with the notes closed; lesson completion alone is not mastery.

Tu auras une réponse par courriel lundi.tü oh-RAH ün ray-POHNS par koo-ree-EHL luh(n)-DEEYou will get an answer by email on Monday.
S'il y a un conflit, nous choisirons un autre horaire.seel ee ah uh(n) kohn-FLEE noo shwah-zee-ROH(N) uh(n) ohtr oh-REHRIf there is a conflict, we will choose another schedule.
Je ne vais pas participer mardi, mais je vais rattraper le cours.zhuh nuh veh pah pahr-tee-see-PAY mahr-DEE meh zhuh veh rah-trah-PAY luh koorI am not going to take part Tuesday, but I am going to catch up on the class.

5.3 Grammar: near-future plans and common future forms (g28)

French often uses the present tense of aller plus an infinitive for an intention. The futur simple also expresses plans, schedules and predictions. These forms can overlap; the choice does not mean simply 'soon versus far away'.

A. Near future: present of aller + infinitive

Keep the second verb in the infinitive: vais suivre, not vais suivrai.
PersonForm of allerPlan with suivre
jevaisje vais suivre un cours
tuvastu vas suivre un cours
il / elle / onvaelle va suivre un cours
nousallonsnous allons suivre un cours
vousallezvous allez suivre un cours
ils / ellesvontils vont suivre un cours
  • Use the present of aller with a second verb in the infinitive: nous allons vérifier; elle va participer.
  • This pattern is common in everyday speech for a plan or intention. It can refer to a later date too; the date alone does not decide the tense.
  • In your notes, change the subject in one plan: je vais suivre → nous allons suivre. Close the notes and rebuild the sentence.
READING 5.3A
Use the pattern
Choose the form that matches the meaning.
1. Choose the correct near-future form: Demain, nous ___ le dossier.
A allons préparerB préparons allonsC irons préparerD va préparer
2. Complete: Cette semaine, elle ___ au test de niveau.
A va participerB participera vaC participerD vont participer

B. Futur simple: endings and common stems

Add -ai, -as, -a, -ons, -ez, -ont. For an -re verb, drop the final e before adding the ending.
Subject-er: préparer-ir: choisir-re: répondre
jeprépareraichoisirairépondrai
tuprépareraschoisirasrépondras
il / ellepréparerachoisirarépondra
nousprépareronschoisironsrépondrons
vouspréparerezchoisirezrépondrez
ils / ellespréparerontchoisirontrépondront
These common verbs use a special stem but keep the same six endings.
InfinitiveStemje / tu / il, ellenous / vous / ils, elles
êtreser-serai / seras / seraserons / serez / seront
avoiraur-aurai / auras / auraaurons / aurez / auront
avoir lieuaur- + lieuaura lieuauront lieu
allerir-irai / iras / irairons / irez / iront
fairefer-ferai / feras / feraferons / ferez / feront
  • For most verbs, start with the infinitive and add -ai, -as, -a, -ons, -ez, -ont. With -re verbs, remove the last e: répondre → répondr-.
  • Learn the common future stems together: être → ser-; avoir → aur-; aller → ir-; faire → fer-.
  • A plan can use either form: je vais suivre un cours and je suivrai un cours are both possible. Near future is especially common in conversation; futur simple is common for schedules, written plans and predictions.
  • In a basic future condition, keep present after si: si le groupe est complet, je choisirai un autre horaire. Do not say si le groupe sera complet.
  • Make a six-person ladder in your notes for one regular verb, then retrieve one irregular stem without looking. Use your own real schedule in a new sentence.
READING 5.3B
Use the pattern
Choose the form that matches the meaning.
1. Complete: Vendredi, je ___ les documents.
A prépareraiB préparerasC prépareD vais préparerai
2. What is the futur simple form of être with je?
A je seraiB je êtreaiC je suisD je vais serai
3. Choose the natural sentence: If the class is full, I will choose another time.
A Si le cours est complet, je choisirai un autre horaire.B Si le cours sera complet, je choisirai un autre horaire.C Si le cours complet, choisirai horaire.D Si le cours est complet, je choisir un autre horaire.
4. Which sentence has répondre in the futur simple with nous?
A Nous répondrons demain.B Nous répondons demain.C Nous répondre demain.D Nous répondra demain.

5.4 Dialogues: fit a French class around a work schedule

Track the current schedule, the new plan and the next action. The speakers check a real constraint before deciding; the learner must use the schedule details rather than guess.

A course after work
Nadia asks a training-centre adviser about evening French classes.
NadiaJe vais suivre un cours de français le mois prochain.I am going to take a French class next month.
ConseillèreVous préférez une formation du soir ou un cours intensif ?Do you prefer an evening course or an intensive class?
NadiaUne formation du soir. Je travaille jusqu'à dix-sept heures.An evening course. I work until five p.m.
ConseillèreLe groupe commence mardi à dix-huit heures. Vous êtes libre à cette heure ?The group starts Tuesday at six p.m. Are you free at that time?
NadiaOui, mais j'ai un autre rendez-vous le premier mardi.Yes, but I have another appointment on the first Tuesday.
ConseillèreVous pouvez rattraper cette séance jeudi. Je vais vous envoyer le formulaire.You can catch up on that class Thursday. I am going to send you the form.
NadiaMerci. Je vais vérifier mon horaire, puis je vais valider mon inscription.Thank you. I am going to check my schedule, then I am going to confirm my registration.
ConseillèreTrès bien. Le groupe sera bientôt complet. La date limite est vendredi.Very good. The group will be full soon. The deadline is Friday.
An interview time conflict
Sam tells his classmate Nadia about a possible conflict.
SamL'entretien d'embauche est jeudi à quinze heures.The job interview is Thursday at three p.m.
NadiaTu vas demander une autre heure ?Are you going to ask for another time?
SamOui. J'ai un quart de jour jeudi, mais je serai libre vendredi matin.Yes. I have a day shift Thursday, but I will be free Friday morning.
NadiaTu vas envoyer un courriel aujourd'hui ?Are you going to send an email today?
SamOui, je vais expliquer le conflit d'horaire et proposer vendredi.Yes, I am going to explain the schedule conflict and suggest Friday.
NadiaEt si vendredi n'est pas possible ?And if Friday is not possible?
SamJe vais demander un rendez-vous la semaine prochaine.I am going to ask for an appointment next week.
NadiaD'accord. Tu auras une réponse la semaine prochaine.All right. You will get an answer next week.
READING 5.4
Follow the exchange
Choose the detail each speaker gives.
1. When does the evening course begin?
A Tuesday at 6 p.m.B Thursday at 5 p.m.C Friday at 9 a.m.D Monday at noon
2. What will Mina do before confirming her registration?
A Check her work scheduleB Send a transcriptC Cancel the courseD Choose an intensive course
3. Why does Sam want to move the interview?
A He has a day shift at the scheduled time.B He will start a French course.C He has no internet access.D His application is incomplete.
4. If Friday does not work, what will Sam do?
A Ask for an appointment next weekB Cancel his applicationC Call his managerD Send his transcript

5.5 Story: a plan that fits a changing week

Read the calendar first, then follow the learner's plan and the centre's next step. After reading, close the lesson and explain the plan from memory using different dates or times.

STORY
Le cours du soir de Nadia
Nadia's evening class
Nadia travaille dans un centre de formation.
Nadia works at a training centre.
Le mois prochain, elle va suivre un cours de français après son travail.
Next month, she is going to take a French class after work.
Le cours a lieu le mardi et le jeudi, de dix-huit heures à vingt heures.
The class meets on Tuesdays and Thursdays, from six to eight p.m.
Elle prépare aussi une candidature pour un stage dans un bureau.
She is also preparing an application for an internship in an office.
La date limite est le quinze novembre; elle va soumettre son dossier vendredi.
The deadline is November fifteenth; she is going to submit her application on Friday.
Si le groupe est complet, elle choisira une autre formation du soir.
If the group is full, she will choose another evening course.
La conseillère va écrire à Nadia quand l'horaire sera confirmé.
The adviser is going to write to Nadia when the schedule is confirmed.
Nadia aura deux cours par semaine et un jour de travail.
Nadia will have two classes per week and one work day.

Understand the story

2. When is the application deadline?
A November 15B November 5C December 15D October 15

A An emailB A paper certificateC A new applicationD A transcript

5.7 Reading: scan a course notice and application message

First identify the document type. Then scan for the date, requirement or next action asked about; do not infer that an application is accepted just because it was received.

5.8 Writing: write about an upcoming schedule change

WRITING 5.8W
Message a classmate about your plan
Write 60-90 French words to a classmate. Explain your study or work plan for next month, give one date or time, describe a schedule problem and suggest a next step. Use at least one near-future form and one futur simple form. This is a guided practice task, not the full official TCF writing task.
Check your text:
  • State the plan and include a concrete date or time.
  • Use aller + infinitive for one plan and a futur simple form for another future action.
  • Explain the schedule problem and give a realistic next step.
  • Check future endings and keep present tense after si in a condition.
  • Count 60-90 words, then close your notes and write a changed plan tomorrow.

5.9 Speaking: explain a plan and adapt it

SPEAKING 5.9S
Plan a course around your week
Tell Marie about a course, work or personal goal for next month. Give two schedule details, one reason for your choice and one possible change. Marie asks what you will do if the schedule conflicts with work. After feedback, make a different plan without reading your notes.
  • Name the goal first, then give dates, times or a schedule condition.
  • Use aller + infinitive for an intended action and a future simple form for what will happen next.
  • Say why the plan suits your week; do not list dates without explaining the choice.
  • On the retry, change the goal and schedule, and answer Marie's follow-up without notes.

A2 Unit 5: retrieve, then transfer

  • After one day, close your notes and retrieve five useful words and the grammar pattern.
  • After three and seven days, use the same language in a changed scene; bring back items that still need effort.
  • Record one self-correction and one original sentence in your notes, then say the sentence without reading.
  • Move on when you can do the transfer task with the notes closed; lesson completion alone is not mastery.

A2.6
Choose a home and describe a neighbourhood
AFTER THIS UNIT YOU CAN

THE SITUATION
A newcomer compares two fictional rental notices in an Ontario city and asks a housing contact about features and monthly costs.
In Canada Rental terms and what utilities include vary by province, landlord and listing. The two notices in this lesson are fictional; do not treat them as current offers or legal advice. A fluent Canadian French reviewer should confirm laveuse, sécheuse, stationnement and bail in context.

6.2 Phrases: compare options and explain a trade-off

In your notes, draw two columns for two homes. Compare price, location and one feature; then close the notes and recommend one option for a person with a specific need.

Useful connected phrases

Le loyer est moins élevé, mais le trajet est plus long.luh lwah-YAY eh mwan ay-luh-VAY meh luh trah-ZHAY eh plü lohnThe rent is lower, but the trip is longer.
Cet appartement offre le meilleur accès au train.seht ah-pahr-tuh-MAH(N) ofr luh meh-YUHR ahk-SEH oh tra(n)This apartment offers the best access to the train.
Pour moi, le coût mensuel est le critère le plus important.poor mwah luh koo mah(n)-sü-EHL eh luh kree-TEHR luh plü a(n)-por-TAH(N)For me, the monthly cost is the most important factor.

6.3 Grammar: compare homes, features and quantities (g29)

Use comparison forms to show how two things differ or match. An adjective describes a noun; a quantity phrase counts a resource. The comparison word goes before the adjective or quantity phrase.

A. Comparatives and superlatives

Que means 'than / as ... as'. Match the adjective ending to the noun: une pièce plus grande; des fenêtres plus grandes.
JobPatternExample
More / lessplus / moins + adjective + queLe studio est moins cher que l'appartement d'une chambre.
Equalaussi + adjective + queLe logement A est aussi lumineux que le logement B.
More / fewerplus / moins de + noun + queLe logement B a plus de rangement que le studio.
The most / leastle / la / les plus / moins + adjectiveC'est le quartier le plus calme.
Better / the bestun meilleur + noun / le, la, les meilleur(e)(s) + nounLe logement B offre un meilleur accès au train. / C'est le meilleur choix.
  • To compare two things, use plus ... que (more than), moins ... que (less than) or aussi ... que (as ... as).
  • With a noun quantity, use plus de / moins de + noun: plus de rangement; moins de bruit. Do not add un after de in this pattern.
  • For a superlative, add the correct article before plus or moins: le plus calme; la moins chère; les plus grands. The adjective agrees with its noun.
  • Meilleur(e)(s) is the irregular comparative of bon: un meilleur accès means 'better access'. For 'the best', add the matching article: le meilleur accès; la meilleure option; les meilleurs choix. Use mieux with a verb; that distinction is for recognition here.
  • Compare like with like and state the criterion. In your notes, write one real comparison for price, one for location and one for a feature; then make a recommendation without reading.
READING 6.3A
Use the pattern
Choose the form that matches the meaning.
1. Complete: Le studio A est ___ cher que le logement B.
A moinsB aussi deC le moins deD meilleur
2. Choose the correct quantity comparison.
A Le logement B a plus de rangement que le studio.B Le logement B a plus rangement que le studio.C Le logement B a plus du rangement que le studio.D Le logement B a plus de rangements que le studio est.
3. Choose the natural superlative: Which room is the brightest?
A C'est la pièce la plus lumineuse.B C'est le pièce le plus lumineux.C C'est la pièce plus lumineuse que.D C'est la plus pièce lumineuse.
4. Complete: This apartment has better access to the train. Cet appartement offre un ___ accès au train.
A meilleurB mieuxC plus bonD meilleure

6.4 Dialogues: ask a housing contact about two listings

Listen for the learner's needs, the details in each listing and the question that remains unanswered. A good comparison uses evidence from the notice instead of guessing about a home.

Two rental options
Lina asks her classmate Karim to help compare two fictional listings.
LinaJ'ai deux annonces. Le studio est moins cher, mais l'appartement d'une chambre est plus près du train.I have two listings. The studio is less expensive, but the one-bedroom is closer to the train.
KarimQuel est ton budget maximal ?What is your maximum budget?
LinaMille quatre cents dollars par mois, avec les frais d'électricité.One thousand four hundred dollars a month, including electricity costs.
KarimLe studio coûte mille trois cent cinquante dollars. Les charges sont comprises.The studio costs thirteen hundred and fifty dollars. Utilities are included.
LinaEt l'appartement d'une chambre ?And the one-bedroom?
KarimIl coûte mille quatre cent cinquante dollars. Il a un balcon privé et plus d'espace de rangement.It costs fourteen hundred and fifty dollars. It has a private balcony and more storage space.
LinaJe préfère un quartier paisible et un trajet court. Le studio est aussi près de la bibliothèque.I prefer a peaceful neighbourhood and a short trip. The studio is also close to the library.
KarimDonc, visite les deux et vérifie le coût mensuel avant de choisir.So visit both and check the monthly cost before choosing.
Ask before you sign
Nadia phones a housing contact to clarify a listing.
NadiaBonjour. Je téléphone au sujet du logement meublé près du centre communautaire.Hello. I am calling about the furnished home near the community centre.
ContactOui. Quelle est votre question ?Yes. What is your question?
NadiaLa buanderie est-elle dans l'immeuble ?Is the laundry room in the building?
ContactOui, elle est au sous-sol. La laveuse et la sécheuse sont dans la buanderie.Yes, it is in the basement. The washer and dryer are in the laundry room.
NadiaLe stationnement intérieur est-il compris dans le loyer ?Is indoor parking included in the rent?
ContactNon. Il y a un coût mensuel en plus.No. There is an extra monthly cost.
NadiaMerci. Je vais visiter le logement samedi et lire les conditions avant de signer un bail.Thank you. I am going to visit the home Saturday and read the conditions before signing a lease.
ContactTrès bien. Le logement est moins bruyant le matin que le soir.Very good. The home is less noisy in the morning than in the evening.

6.8 Writing: write an inquiry that compares what you need

WRITING 6.8W
Ask about a rental listing
Write 60-90 French words to a housing contact. Ask about two features, explain your budget or travel priority, and compare the listing with one other option. Use two comparison forms. This is an original guided message, not an official TCF task.
Check your text:
  • Ask specific questions that the notice does not answer.
  • Compare two homes on the same criterion and use que correctly.
  • Use a superlative only when comparing a set of at least three or all available options.
  • Count 60-90 words, then reread the adjective endings and monthly amounts.
  • Close your notes tomorrow and write a new inquiry for a different home.

6.9 Speaking: recommend a home with evidence

SPEAKING 6.9S
Choose between two apartments
You have two rental notices and a monthly budget. Tell Marie which home you would visit first, compare price and travel, and ask one question that the notice does not answer. Marie changes one preference; reconsider your choice without reading your notes.
  • Say the criterion before the comparison so the listener can follow your reasoning.
  • Use figures from the notices and give a reason for your recommendation.
  • Ask one practical question that is not answered in the listing.
  • On the retry, respond to Marie's changed need and use a different comparison.

A2 Unit 6: retrieve, then transfer

  • After one day, close your notes and retrieve five useful words and the grammar pattern.
  • After three and seven days, use the same language in a changed scene; bring back items that still need effort.
  • Record one self-correction and one original sentence in your notes, then say the sentence without reading.
  • Move on when you can do the transfer task with the notes closed; lesson completion alone is not mastery.
A2 · UNIT 7
A2.7
Health appointments and getting help
AFTER THIS UNIT YOU CAN
Describe a health concern without diagnosing it, give useful timing details, ask for clarification and combine familiar information with qui, que and où.
THE SITUATION
A learner contacts a community clinic about a new concern, completes an intake form and confirms what information to bring.
In Canada Health-service routes, fees and referral steps differ by province and territory. The clinic and examples here are fictional and are not medical advice. For a real health concern, use the official provincial or territorial health service; urgent or severe symptoms need prompt help from local emergency services.

7.2 Phrases: combine details instead of repeating a noun

7.3 Grammar: relative pronouns qui, que and où (g30)

A relative pronoun joins information about a person, thing, place or time. It replaces a repeated noun and links the extra detail to the first part of the sentence.

A. Choose the pronoun by its job in the second clause

After qui, a verb follows. After que / qu', a subject usually follows. Où refers to a place or time.
PronounJob in its clauseTwo ideas → one sentence
quiSubject: it does the verbC'est le service. Le service répond. → C'est le service qui répond.
que / qu'Direct object: the verb acts on itVoici le formulaire. La clinique demande le formulaire. → Voici le formulaire que la clinique demande.
oùPlace or time: there / whenC'est la clinique. J'ai mon rendez-vous là. → C'est la clinique où j'ai mon rendez-vous.
quiPerson as subjectJe parle au professionnel. Le professionnel suit mon dossier. → Je parle au professionnel qui suit mon dossier.
que / qu'Thing as direct objectJe garde le message. Le service a envoyé le message. → Je garde le message que le service a envoyé.
  • Use qui when the missing noun is the subject of the next verb: le service répond → le service qui répond.
  • Use que when the next clause already has a subject and the missing noun receives the action: la clinique demande le formulaire → le formulaire que la clinique demande.
  • Before a vowel sound, que shortens to qu': la question qu'elle pose. The role stays the same.
  • Use où for a place or time: la clinique où je consulte; le jour où le service rappelle.
  • Ask what follows the blank: does the noun do the verb (qui), receive it (que), or name a place/time (où)? Use that test in your notes with a clinic, a form and a date.
READING 7.3A
Use the pattern
Choose the form that matches the meaning.
1. C'est le formulaire ___ la clinique demande.
A queB quiC oùD quand
2. C'est le professionnel ___ explique l'étape.
A quiB queC oùD qu'
3. C'est le centre ___ j'ai mon rendez-vous.
A oùB quiC queD qu'
4. Je lis la note ___ elle a envoyée.
A qu'B quiC oùD dont

7.4 Dialogues: explain a concern and confirm the next step

The receptionist does not diagnose. Listen for the duration, the kind of appointment offered and the information the learner needs to prepare.

Call the clinic
Awa calls a fictional community clinic about a new concern.
AwaBonjour. Je téléphone au sujet d'un symptôme nouveau.Hello. I am calling about a new symptom.
RéceptionnisteDepuis quand ?Since when?
AwaDepuis deux jours. J'ai aussi une légère sensation de brûlure après le repas.For two days. I also have a mild burning sensation after a meal.
RéceptionnisteJe peux noter l'information. Un professionnel va consulter votre dossier.I can write down the information. A health professional is going to consult your file.
AwaEst-ce qu'il y a une consultation téléphonique ?Is there a phone consultation?
RéceptionnisteOui. Le service de triage va vous rappeler aujourd'hui.Yes. The triage service is going to call you back today.
AwaQuel document dois-je préparer ?Which document should I prepare?
RéceptionnisteVotre numéro de dossier et la liste de vos questions.Your file number and your list of questions.
Confirm the appointment
Karim confirms details for a follow-up appointment.
KarimJ'ai un rendez-vous de suivi à la clinique demain.I have a follow-up appointment at the clinic tomorrow.
RéceptionnisteC'est une consultation en personne ?Is it an in-person appointment?
KarimOui, à dix heures. Est-ce que je dois apporter le résultat de laboratoire ?Yes, at ten. Should I bring the lab result?
RéceptionnisteVous pouvez apporter le résultat de laboratoire.You can bring the lab result.
KarimJe vais noter les consignes. La clinique est près du centre communautaire, c'est bien ça ?I will write down the instructions. The clinic is near the community centre, is that right?
RéceptionnisteOui. Le message de confirmation indique l'adresse et l'heure.Yes. The confirmation message gives the address and time.
KarimMerci. Je vais relire le message avant de partir.Thank you. I will reread the message before leaving.

7.8 Writing: write a clear message before an appointment

WRITING 7.8W
Contact a clinic with useful details
Write 60-90 French words to a fictional clinic. Give a neutral description, when it began, one timing detail and one clear question. Use one sentence with qui, que or où. Do not diagnose yourself or suggest a treatment; this is language practice, not a medical message template for urgent care.
Check your text:
  • State the concern neutrally; do not write a diagnosis.
  • Give the duration and one specific time or frequency detail.
  • Ask one clear question about a document or next step.
  • Check whether qui, que or où matches the job in the second clause.
  • Count 60-90 words and close your notes before rewriting a different message.

7.9 Speaking: explain a concern and ask a follow-up question

SPEAKING 7.9S
Prepare for a clinic call
Tell Marie what information you would give a clinic receptionist: when the concern began, when it happens and what appointment you are requesting. Marie asks which document or next step you need to clarify. Use one relative clause, then repeat the explanation with a different timeline.
  • Give timing and observable details, not a diagnosis.
  • Say what appointment or information you need; a receptionist may route the request.
  • Use qui for the person or service doing the action, que for the thing receiving it, and où for a place or time.
  • On the retry, change the concern and timeline and answer Marie's follow-up without notes.

A2 Unit 7: retrieve, then transfer

  • After one day, close your notes and retrieve five useful words and the grammar pattern.
  • After three and seven days, use the same language in a changed scene; bring back items that still need effort.
  • Record one self-correction and one original sentence in your notes, then say the sentence without reading.
  • Move on when you can do the transfer task with the notes closed; lesson completion alone is not mastery.

8.2 Phrases: replace a repeated document with a short pronoun

First say the document name; then replace it with le, la or les. In your notes, write two versions of a work or study sentence, close the notes, and say the short version without losing which document it refers to.

Useful connected phrases
FrenchSay itEnglishIn a sentence
Le compte rendu ? Je le relis avant midi.luh koh(n)t ruh(n)-DÜ zhuh luh ruh-LEEZ ah-vah(n) mee-DEEThe report? I am rereading it before noon.
La version finale ? Je la corrige aujourd'hui.lah vehr-SYOH(N) fee-NAHL zhuh lah koh-REEZH oh-zhoor-DWEEThe final version? I am correcting it today.
Les documents ? Nous les classons par date.lay doh-kü-MAH(N) noo lay klah-SOH(N) par dahtThe documents? We are filing them by date.
Le fichier joint ? Je vais le télécharger après le cours.luh fee-SHYAY zhWA(N) zhuh veh luh day-shahr-ZHAY ah-preh luh koorThe attached file? I am going to download it after class.
La présentation ? Sam va la préparer avec l'équipe.lah pray-zah(n)-tah-SYOH(N) sahm vah lah pray-pah-RAY ah-vehk lay-KEEPThe presentation? Sam is going to prepare it with the team.
Les corrections ? Je les note dans le brouillon.lay koh-rehk-SYOH(N) zhuh lay noht dah(n) luh broo-YOH(N)The corrections? I am writing them down in the draft.
Le document ? Je ne le trouve pas dans le dossier.luh doh-kü-MAH(N) zhuh nuh luh troov pah dah(n) luh doh-SYAYThe document? I cannot find it in the file.
La note ? Je vais l'envoyer au tuteur.lah noht zhuh veh lah(n)-vwah-YAY oh tü-TUHRThe note? I am going to send it to the supervisor.

8.3 Grammar: direct object pronouns le, la and les (g31)

A direct object answers 'what?' or 'whom?' after the verb. If the noun is already clear, French can replace it with le, la or les. The pronoun normally sits before the verb it belongs to.

A. Choose the pronoun, then place it before its verb

Notice the verb forms in project messages. The object pronoun then replaces the repeated document: Nadia le relit; le comité la reçoit.
Useful formMeaning in this scene
La formatrice demande le plan.The instructor asks for the outline.
Tu remets le fichier jeudi ?Are you handing in the file Thursday?
Je joins la liste au dossier.I attach the list to the file.
Tu envoies le compte rendu ?Are you sending the report?
Nadia relit le compte rendu.Nadia rereads the report.
Sam partage la version.Sam shares the version.
Le comité reçoit la version finale.The committee receives the final version.
Nadia a relu le document.Nadia reread the document.
Les documents sont prêts.The documents are ready.
Le plan doit être prêt vendredi.The outline must be ready Friday.
The pronoun matches the noun replaced, not the speaker. Before a vowel sound, le and la shorten to l'.
Original nounPronounWith a conjugated verbWith aller + infinitive
le compte renduleJe le relis.Je vais le relire.
la versionlaJe la corrige.Je vais la corriger.
les documentslesNous les classons.Nous allons les classer.
l'ordre du jourle → l'Je l'organise.Je vais l'organiser.
  • Use le for one masculine direct object, la for one feminine direct object, and les for plural objects of any gender.
  • Place the pronoun before a conjugated verb: je le relis; elle la corrige; nous les classons.
  • With aller + infinitive, place it before the infinitive: je vais le relire; nous allons les classer.
  • Before a vowel sound, le and la become l': la note → je l'envoie. The noun's gender is still the way you choose the pronoun.
  • With a simple negative, ne and pas go around the conjugated verb: je ne le trouve pas. In your notes, replace the same noun in three sentences; then retrieve them without looking.
READING 8.3A
Use the pattern
Choose the form that matches the meaning.
1. Le compte rendu ? Je ___ relis.
A leB laC lesD lui
2. La version ? Nous ___ corrigeons aujourd'hui.
A laB leC lesD lui
3. Les fichiers ? Sam va ___ classer par date.
A lesB leC laD lui
4. Le plan ? Je vais ___ organiser demain.
A l'B laC lesD lui

Track which person owns each task and which document the pronouns replace. If you lose the reference, ask a short clarification question before continuing.

8.8 Writing: write a clear work or study follow-up

WRITING 8.8W
Confirm who will handle each document
Write 60-90 French words to a classmate or colleague. Name two project documents, say who will review or send each one and give a deadline. Use le, la or les at least three times with clear references. This is an original practice message, not an official TCF task.
Check your text:
  • Name the noun before using a pronoun so its reference is clear.
  • Match le, la or les to the noun's gender and number.
  • Place the pronoun before the conjugated verb or before the infinitive after aller.
  • Give each person a clear action and include a deadline.
  • Count 60-90 words and rewrite the message with two different documents tomorrow.

8.9 Speaking: coordinate and clarify a document hand-off

SPEAKING 8.9S
Tell Marie who will review each item
Explain a small work or class project to Marie. Name two documents, say who will review or send each one and when it is due. Use le, la and les with clear references. Marie asks which document you mean once; clarify and retry with a different project.
  • Say the document name first when a listener might not know what le, la or les refers to.
  • Keep each pronoun close to its verb: je la corrige; je vais la corriger.
  • Use the noun's gender and number, not the gender of the person doing the task.
  • On the retry, change the documents and the deadline and clarify one reference without reading.

A2 Unit 8: retrieve, then transfer

  • After one day, close your notes and retrieve five useful words and the grammar pattern.
  • After three and seven days, use the same language in a changed scene; bring back items that still need effort.
  • Record one self-correction and one original sentence in your notes, then say the sentence without reading.
  • Move on when you can do the transfer task with the notes closed; lesson completion alone is not mastery.
A2 · UNIT 9
A2.9
Travel across the city and beyond
AFTER THIS UNIT YOU CAN
Ask for travel information, give or request something from a person, refer back to a place with y, and explain a simple route change.
THE SITUATION
Nadia and Karim travel to a community event. A service interruption changes their route, so they ask for details and confirm where to go.

9.3 Grammar: lui / leur and y for people and places (g32-g33)

French can avoid repeating a person or a place. First identify what the verb is connected to: lui or leur refers to a person after a verb used with à; y refers to a previously named place with common location patterns.

A. Give or ask something to a person: lui and leur

Read these useful travel forms aloud, then reuse one in a new route. They support the role-play; the target grammar remains the person and place pronouns.
Useful formMeaning
La ligne est interrompue aujourd'hui.The line is interrupted today.
Le service est interrompu aujourd'hui.Service is interrupted today.
Tu demandes le quai à Nadia ?Are you asking Nadia for the platform?
Nous manquons la correspondance.We are missing the connection.
Tu donnes l'adresse à Karim ?Are you giving Karim the address?
Les voyageurs lisent l'avis.The travellers are reading the notice.
Le service habituel ne passe pas; une autre ligne le remplace.The usual service does not run; another line replaces it.
Ils attendent au carrefour.They are waiting at the intersection.
Ils montent dans le train et rejoignent le groupe.They get on the train and meet the group.
Lui is singular for a man or a woman. Leur is plural. Both go before the conjugated verb; with aller + infinitive, put the pronoun before the infinitive.
Full phrasePerson pronounShort sentence
Je demande le quai à Nadia.à Nadia → luiJe lui demande le quai.
Je donne l'adresse à Karim.à Karim → luiJe lui donne l'adresse.
Je donne le plan aux voyageurs.aux voyageurs → leurJe leur donne le plan.
Je vais expliquer le trajet à Nadia.à Nadia → luiJe vais lui expliquer le trajet.
  • Use lui for one person and leur for more than one person when the taught verb takes à before that person: demander à, donner à, expliquer à.
  • The pronoun replaces the person, not the thing: in Je lui donne l'adresse, lui means the person and l'adresse is what is given.
  • Place it before the verb: je lui demande; je leur réponds. With a near-future verb: je vais lui demander.
  • Do not choose lui by gender. One man or one woman both use lui; a group uses leur.
READING 9.3A
Use the pattern
Choose the form that matches the meaning.
1. Je donne le plan à Nadia. Je ___ donne le plan.
A luiB leurC yD les
2. Je vais expliquer la route aux voyageurs. Je vais ___ expliquer la route.
A leurB luiC yD la
3. What does lui replace in Je lui demande le quai?
A The person being askedB The platformC The stationD The route

B. Refer back to a place: y

In this unit, y refers to a named place with aller or être. It goes before a conjugated verb, or before the infinitive when two verbs are used.
Place named firstShort formMeaning
Nous allons à la gare.Nous y allons.We are going there.
Nadia est au terminus.Nadia y est.Nadia is there.
Tu vas au centre communautaire ?Tu y vas ?Are you going there?
Je vais retourner à la bibliothèque.Je vais y retourner.I am going to return there.
  • Name the place first, then use y to mean 'there': à la gare → nous y allons.
  • Place y before its verb: j'y vais; elle y est. With a second verb, place it before the infinitive: je vais y retourner.
  • In these patterns, y refers to a place, not a person. Compare je lui parle (to that person) and j'y vais (to that place).
  • This unit practises y with common location patterns. Do not assume it replaces every phrase beginning with à.
READING 9.3B
Use the pattern
Choose the form that matches the meaning.
1. Nous allons à la bibliothèque. Nous ___ allons après le cours.
A yB luiC leurD la
2. Nadia est au terminus. Nadia ___ est.
A yB leurC lesD lui
3. Which sentence refers to a person?
A Je lui demande le quai.B J'y vais.C Elle y est.D Nous y retournons.

9.6 Listening: catch the destination and the next step

Hear each original message once. Note the person who has the information, the named place and the change to the route. These are fictional announcements, not current service notices.

LISTENING 9.6
Listen for meaning
Each original recording is played once. Listen for the goal and one useful detail.
1. Who should a traveller ask?
