9.8 Writing: write a route-change message

WRITING 9.8W
Tell someone how you will get there
Write 60-90 French words to a classmate. Say where you are, name a service or route change, explain where you will go next, and ask one person for information. Use lui or leur and y at least once. Give a clear time or meeting point; use an invented route.
Check your text:
  • Name the person or place before replacing it with a pronoun.
  • Use lui/leur for the person in the patterns taught here; use y for a named place.
  • Put the pronoun before its verb, including before the infinitive after aller.
  • Make the route and meeting point easy to follow.
  • Write 60-90 words, then close your notes and explain the route aloud.

9.9 Speaking: ask for details and adapt the route

SPEAKING 9.9S
Help Marie find the meeting point
Marie knows the destination but not the changed route. Ask her for one missing detail, explain where you are going, and confirm the meeting point. Use lui/leur and y where they fit. On the retry, use a new destination and a different service change without a prepared question list.
  • Say the place name once before using y.
  • Say who receives the question or information before using lui or leur if the listener might lose the reference.
  • Ask one specific follow-up, such as the time or stop, rather than guessing.
  • On the second attempt, change the route and explain the change with your notes closed.

A2 Unit 9: retrieve, then transfer

  • After one day, close your notes and retrieve five useful words and the grammar pattern.
  • After three and seven days, use the same language in a changed scene; bring back items that still need effort.
  • Record one self-correction and one original sentence in your notes, then say the sentence without reading.
  • Move on when you can do the transfer task with the notes closed; lesson completion alone is not mastery.
A2 · UNIT 10
A2.10
Food, shopping and quantities
AFTER THIS UNIT YOU CAN
Ask for and compare quantities, keep the amount clear with en, explain a food preference and understand simple price or product information.
THE SITUATION
Karim plans a shared meal, checks the amount and ingredients, and chooses between two shopping options without buying more than he needs.

Confirm the unit and amount when needed. Store return policies vary by business. Store return and refund policies vary by business. This lesson teaches language, not consumer or nutrition advice.

10.2 Phrases: keep the quantity, replace the food

Say the full phrase, then shorten it with en. Write one personal shopping example in your notes and change only the amount; the listener should still understand what you mean.

Useful connected phrases
FrenchSay itEnglishIn a sentence
Des fraises ? J'en prends une barquette.day frehz; zhah(n) prahn ün bah-REHTStrawberries? I am taking a punnet.
Du lait végétal ? J'en prends un litre.dü leh vay-zhay-TAHL; zhah(n) prahn uh(n) leetrPlant-based milk? I am taking a litre of it.
Tu veux des lentilles ? Oui, j'en veux.tü vuh day lah(n)-TEE-yuh; wee, zhah(n) vuhDo you want lentils? Yes, I want some.
Combien de yaourts ? J'en prends quatre.koh(n)-BYAH(N) duh yah-OOR; zhah(n) prahn kahtrHow many yogurts? I am taking four.
Il reste du pain ? Oui, il en reste la moitié.eel rehst dü pa(n); wee, eel ah(n) rehst ün mwah-TYAYIs there bread left? Yes, half of it is left.
Je vais acheter des tomates; je vais en prendre six.zhuh veh ahsh-TAY day toh-MAHT; zhuh veh ah(n) prahn-druh seesI am going to buy tomatoes; I will take six.
Tu prends du fromage ? J'en prends un morceau.tü prahn dü froh-MAHZH; zhah(n) prahn uh(n) mohr-SOHAre you taking cheese? I am taking a piece.
Ce produit est trop cher. Je n'en prends pas.suh proh-DÜEE eh troh shehr; zhuh nah(n) prahn pahThis product is too expensive. I am not taking any.

10.3 Grammar: en for an amount or a de-phrase (g34)

When the food or item is already clear, en can replace a phrase with de or a quantity phrase. Keep the amount after the verb when the amount matters: des pommes → j'en prends; un kilo de pommes → j'en prends un kilo.

A. Replace the item and keep the amount

Read each quantity and its unit together. This helps you understand the amount before you replace the food with en.
Useful formMeaning
Un paquet suffit.One packet is enough.
Je prends deux barquettes de fraises.I am taking two punnets of strawberries.
Nous gardons la moitié pour demain.We are keeping half for tomorrow.
Je réchauffe le plat préparé.I am reheating the prepared dish.
Une portion individuelle est prévue.An individual serving is planned.
Place en before the verb it belongs to. With aller + infinitive, put it before the infinitive: je vais en acheter. Keep a useful amount or number after the verb.
Full phraseShort formWhat stays
Je prends des lentilles.J'en prends.The item is clear from context.
Je veux du lait végétal.J'en veux.The item is clear from context.
Je prends une barquette de fraises.J'en prends une barquette.The package amount stays.
Je prends deux yaourts.J'en prends deux.The number stays.
Je vais acheter un kilo de tomates.Je vais en acheter un kilo.The amount stays after the infinitive.
Je prends de la sauce.J'en prends la moitié.The amount stays after the verb.
Je ne prends pas de lentilles.Je n'en prends pas.In the negative, ne comes before en.
  • Use en to avoid repeating a thing introduced with de, du, de la, des, or a quantity phrase: des fraises → j'en prends.
  • Keep the quantity when it carries information: un kilo → j'en prends un kilo; quatre → j'en prends quatre.
  • Put en before the verb: j'en veux; il en reste. With aller + infinitive, put it before the infinitive: je vais en prendre.
  • In a simple negative, use ne + en + verb + pas: je n'en prends pas.
  • Here, practise en with food and quantities. Other uses of en exist; do not infer every one from these examples.
  • Write one sentence about an amount you buy, underline what en replaces, and retrieve it later without looking.
READING 10.3A
Use the pattern
Choose the form that matches the meaning.
1. Tu achètes des fraises ? Oui, j'___ achète.
A enB yC luiD les
2. Je prends une barquette de fraises. Je ___ prends une barquette.
A enB yC leurD la
3. Choose the correct near-future form for 'I will buy one kilogram of tomatoes.'
A Je vais en acheter un kilo.B Je vais acheter en un kilo.C J'en vais acheter un kilo.D Je vais un kilo en acheter.

10.4 Dialogues: choose an amount and check an ingredient

Listen for what each person wants, the amount and the reason for a choice. When a food name is already clear, listen for en and the quantity that follows it.

Plan the shared meal
Karim and Nadia prepare a meal for a small study group.
NadiaCombien de lentilles faut-il pour le repas ?How many lentils are needed for the meal?

10.8 Writing: write a practical meal or shopping message

WRITING 10.8W
Agree on amounts for a shared meal
Write 60-90 French words to a classmate. Suggest two foods and exact quantities, say whether one product fits a preference, and ask the other person to bring one item. Use en at least twice while keeping amounts clear. Use fictional prices if you mention them.
Check your text:
  • Name the food before replacing it with en.
  • Keep the number, package or measurement after en when it matters.
  • Use a clear quantity and make the request easy to act on.
  • Check that a preference or restriction is described neutrally, without making health claims.
  • Write 60-90 words and rewrite once with different foods and amounts.

10.9 Speaking: compare two shopping choices

SPEAKING 10.9S
Choose a basket for the meal
Explain to Marie which two items you will buy and how much of each. Compare one price or package and check that the choice fits the stated preference. Marie changes one item; respond with a new amount using en. Retry with an unfamiliar product and no written model.
  • Say the food once so en has a clear reference.
  • Keep the amount after en: j'en prends deux; j'en prends un paquet.
  • Explain one reason for choosing the product, such as the amount or price information.
  • If Marie changes the item, name it clearly before using en in the new answer.

A2 Unit 10: retrieve, then transfer

  • After one day, close your notes and retrieve five useful words and the grammar pattern.
  • After three and seven days, use the same language in a changed scene; bring back items that still need effort.
  • Record one self-correction and one original sentence in your notes, then say the sentence without reading.
  • Move on when you can do the transfer task with the notes closed; lesson completion alone is not mastery.

11.3 Grammar: simple instructions and polite requests (g35-g36)

A practical request usually has three parts: state the problem, ask for one action and confirm what happens next. French uses the imperative for a direct instruction and the conditional of pouvoir or vouloir to make many requests sound more courteous.

A. Give or follow a short instruction: the imperative

These short messages reuse the lesson's document vocabulary and model one useful instruction.
Service messageMeaning
Le formulaire est incomplet.The form is incomplete.
Il n'y a pas de copie recto verso.There is no double-sided copy.
Il vérifie le formulaire.He checks the form.
Gardez le numéro de confirmation.Keep the confirmation number.
The imperative has no written subject pronoun. Use vous for an unfamiliar service contact or a plural group. With a regular -er verb, the familiar tu form drops final -s: tu coches → coche.
Infinitivetu (familiar)vous (polite or plural)Negative instruction
cocherCoche la case.Cochez la case.Ne cochez pas la mauvaise case.
appuyer surAppuie sur le bouton.Appuyez sur le bouton.N'appuyez pas avant de vérifier.
attacherAttache le reçu.Attachez le reçu.N'attachez pas le mauvais document.
choisirChoisis une option.Choisissez une option.Ne choisissez pas sans vérifier.
  • For an affirmative instruction, use the tu, nous or vous verb form without the subject: coche; cochons; cochez.
  • For a negative instruction, place ne (or n' before a vowel sound) before the verb and pas after it: ne cochez pas; n'appuyez pas.
  • Use tu only when the relationship is familiar. Use vous with an unfamiliar customer-service contact or when speaking to several people.
  • For regular -er verbs, the tu imperative drops final -s: tu coches → coche. Other verbs do not all follow this pattern: tu choisis → choisis.
  • Keep an instruction short and limited to the next action. For a request to another person, the conditional form may be more suitable.
READING 11.3A
Use the pattern
Choose the form that matches the meaning.
1. Choose the polite instruction for an unfamiliar service contact.
A Cochez la case obligatoire.B Coche la case obligatoire.C Tu coches la case obligatoire.D Vous cocher la case obligatoire.
2. Choose the correct negative instruction.
A N'appuyez pas sur le bouton.B Ne appuyez pas sur le bouton.C N'appuyez sur pas le bouton.D N'appuyez pas bouton le sur.
3. What happens to a regular -er verb in the familiar tu imperative?
A The final -s is removed: coche.B The subject tu stays: tu coche.C The verb moves after the object.D The verb takes -ez: cochez.

B. Ask politely with pouvoir and vouloir in the conditional

For pouvoir, vous pourriez means 'could you'; for vouloir, je voudrais means 'I would like'. Keep the infinitive after pourriez or voudriez.
Direct instructionPolite requestPurpose
Vérifiez le numéro.Pourriez-vous vérifier le numéro ?Ask someone to check.
Donnez-moi le délai.Pourriez-vous me donner le délai ?Ask for one clear piece of information.
Je veux une réponse écrite.Je voudrais une réponse écrite.State what you would like.
Confirmez le rendez-vous.Voudriez-vous confirmer le rendez-vous ?Make a courteous request.
  • Use pourriez-vous + infinitive to request an action: Pourriez-vous vérifier le numéro ? The pronoun vous follows the verb in this question form.
  • Use je voudrais + noun to state a request politely: Je voudrais une réponse écrite.
  • A polite request still needs a specific action or item. Add a short reason or context if the other person needs it.
  • The present form can sound direct in a service exchange; the conditional is a useful courteous choice. Tone and register also matter.
READING 11.3B
Use the pattern
Choose the form that matches the meaning.
1. Choose the polite request for someone to check a tracking number.
A Pourriez-vous vérifier le numéro de suivi ?B Pouvez-vous vérifié le numéro de suivi ?C Pourriez vérifier-vous le numéro de suivi ?D Pourriez-vous vérifiez le numéro de suivi ?
2. Choose the polite way to ask for a written reply.
A Je voudrais une réponse écrite.B Je veux une réponse écrite maintenant !C Je voudrais une répondre écrite.D Je voudrais à une réponse écrite.
3. Which phrase asks someone to explain the next step politely?
A Pourriez-vous m'expliquer la prochaine étape ?B Explique-moi la prochaine étape !C Tu expliques la prochaine étape.D Vous expliquer la prochaine étape.

11.8 Writing: write a concise service request

WRITING 11.8W
Explain the problem and ask for a next step
Write 60-90 French words to an unfamiliar service contact. State one problem, give a useful detail such as a tracking or confirmation number, make one polite request with pourriez-vous or je voudrais, and ask when or how you will receive a response. This is an original learning task, not a full official TCF writing task.
Check your text:
  • State one problem clearly and include a useful detail.
  • Make one specific request with a polite conditional form.
  • Ask for one clear next step or time.
  • Use an appropriate greeting and sign-off for an unfamiliar contact.
  • Write 60-90 words, then close the example and retry with a repair or form problem.

11.9 Speaking: make a polite request and confirm the action

SPEAKING 11.9S
Explain a service problem to Marie
Role-play a short exchange with Marie as an unfamiliar service contact. Choose a missing delivery, bill question, incomplete form or repair request. Give one detail, ask for one action politely and confirm what happens next. Marie then changes one condition; adapt without reading a prepared script.
  • Use vous in the service role-play unless the relationship is clearly familiar.
  • Give the problem before asking for an action.
  • Ask one polite question at a time, then listen for the answer.
  • Repeat the next step in your own words so you can confirm it.

11.10 TCF corner and checkpoint

These original short service notices and messages practise following a procedure and identifying a next step. They are not official TCF questions. The writing task here develops Task 1-style clarity but is shorter and scaffolded; it does not claim full exam readiness.

LISTENING 11.10L
TCF listening practice
Listen once; choose the answer supported by the recording.
1. What two things does the speaker request?
A Check the tracking number and confirm the next time slotB Replace the heating and repair the tapC Sign the form and attach a copyD Change the payment and refund the bill
2. What should be attached?
A The double-sided copyB The itemized receiptC The tracking numberD The maintenance appointment
3. When will the service handle the request?
A TomorrowB TodayC On ThursdayD In two days
READING 11.10R
TCF reading practice
Read each short message or notice once for the requested information.
CONFIRMATION - Numéro de commande 8126. Créneau de livraison : vendredi, de 9 h à 11 h.
1. When is the delivery time slot?
A Friday from 9 to 11 a.m.B Thursday from 9 to 11 a.m.C Friday from 11 a.m. to 1 p.m.D Monday from 9 to 11 a.m.
FORMULAIRE - Cochez la case obligatoire. N'appuyez pas sur le bouton avant d'ajouter la copie.
2. When should the reader press the button?
A After adding the copyB Before checking the boxC Before opening the formD After contacting the landlord
SERVICE - Une réponse écrite est prévue dans deux jours. Gardez le numéro de confirmation.
3. What should the reader keep?
A The confirmation numberB The repair manualC The parcelD The payment option
READING 11.10C
Checkpoint: 10 questions
Aim for 8/10. For each missed item, name the clue and retry after a delay.
1. Choose the polite request to check the tracking number.
A Pourriez-vous vérifier le numéro de suivi ?B Pourriez vérifier-vous le numéro de suivi ?C Pourriez-vous vérifiez le numéro de suivi ?D Pouvez-vous vérifié le numéro de suivi ?
2. Choose the correct negative imperative.
A N'appuyez pas sur le bouton.B Ne appuyez pas sur le bouton.C N'appuyez sur pas le bouton.D N'appuyez pas bouton sur le.
3. Choose the familiar instruction for one person using cocher.
A Coche la case.B Coches la case.C Tu coche la case.D Cochez la case.
4. Choose the polite way to say 'I would like a written reply.'
A Je voudrais une réponse écrite.B Je voudrais une répondre écrite.C Je veux une réponse écrite maintenant !D Je voudrais à une réponse écrite.
5. Choose the polite request to send a confirmation.
A Pourriez-vous envoyer la confirmation ?B Pourriez-vous envoyez la confirmation ?C Pourriez envoyer-vous la confirmation ?D Vous pourriez envoyer la confirmation hier ?
6. Which form is appropriate for an unfamiliar service contact?
A Pourriez-vous vérifier le formulaire ?B Vérifie le formulaire !C Tu vérifies le formulaire.D Vérifier le formulaire.
7. Choose the correct instruction to a group: 'Do not sign the incomplete form.'
A Ne signez pas le formulaire incomplet.B Ne signe pas le formulaire incomplet.C Vous ne signez pas le formulaire incomplet.D Ne pas signez le formulaire incomplet.
8. A customer wants an answer by email. Which request is clearest?
A Je voudrais une réponse écrite, s'il vous plaît.B Je veux parler demain et vous faites ça.C La réponse écrite est hier.D Donnez une réponse maintenant !
9. What should a short service request include after the problem?
A A specific action request and a next-step questionB Several unrelated personal storiesC Only a greeting and no requestD A guess about the service's legal duty
10. Which reply best confirms the next step?
A Merci, je confirme : vous envoyez la réponse demain ?B Le formulaire est bleu.C Je ne sais pas, au revoir.D Vous devez tout résoudre maintenant.

A2 Unit 11: retrieve, then transfer

  • After one day, close your notes and retrieve five useful words and the grammar pattern.
  • After three and seven days, use the same language in a changed scene; bring back items that still need effort.
  • Record one self-correction and one original sentence in your notes, then say the sentence without reading.
  • Move on when you can do the transfer task with the notes closed; lesson completion alone is not mastery.
A2 · UNIT 12
A2.12
A2 capstone: handle a changing week
AFTER THIS UNIT YOU CAN
Handle a new, familiar-life situation across listening, reading, speaking and writing; connect past events and future plans; ask for information; explain a choice; and notice what still needs review.
THE SITUATION
A learner's work, study and travel plans change during one week. She gathers details, adapts, asks for help, completes a short exam-style set and reports what she can do independently.
In Canada This capstone uses fictional schedules, services and prices. It checks language across everyday Canadian-life contexts; it does not certify a TCF score or replace a current official practice paper.

12.1 Words: schedule changes, exam clues and self-review

Keep the left side of the iPad lesson visible and use the notes app on the right. First try each scene without copying the model. In your notes, record one phrase, one grammar pattern and one error; close the notes before the final transfer.

12.2 Phrases: repair, confirm and transfer

Use the short phrases as tools, not as scripts. Write one version for your own week. Then change the date, destination and problem and explain the new plan without looking.

12.3 Grammar: retrieve the A2 patterns in a new situation

This unit introduces no new grammar. Use the table to recall a form from Units 1-11, then notice whether you can understand it and produce it independently in a different situation.

A. A2 grammar retrieval map

Use the cue to retrieve, not to reread. If a form is not available without the table, mark it for delayed practice and return to it after another lesson.
TargetRecall cueTransfer example
g23 Connect ideasOrder, contrast and reasonD'abord, je lis l'avis, mais je demande une précision parce que le lieu a changé.
g24 Passé composé: regular formsCompleted action with avoirJ'ai réservé le trajet et j'ai confirmé l'heure.
g25 Passé composé: common patternsIrregular forms with avoir and selected verbs with êtreNadia a lu l'avis et elle est arrivée au centre à l'heure.
g26 Imparfait backgroundPast habit or settingIl pleuvait quand le bus est arrivé.

g34 en for quantityKeep the amountJe prends des pommes; j'en prends quatre.
g35 ImperativeShort positive or negative instructionConfirmez l'heure; ne changez pas le rendez-vous.

  • Before studying the table, cover the right column and try to make one original sentence for each grammar target from memory.
  • Then compare your sentence with the cue. Note a useful correction without copying the entire example.
  • Report listening recognition, reading comprehension, independent speaking and independent writing separately; one strong score does not prove all four skills.
  • Repeat the capstone after a delay with new names, dates, places and a changed problem. Use the same grammar in a new situation.
  • Move on to B1 when the A2 transfer tasks are reliable, not only when every screen is completed. Use the resulting profile to choose the first B1 reviews.

Use the pattern
Choose the form that matches the meaning.
1. Put the completed action in the passé composé: 'Nadia ___ le message.'
A a luB lisaitC va lireD lira
2. Choose the sentence with a background and a completed event.
A Il pleuvait quand le bus est arrivé.B Il a plu quand le bus arrivait demain.C Il pleut quand le bus est arrivé demain.D Il pleuvra quand le bus arrivait.

12.4 Dialogues: adapt a plan and ask one follow-up question

First listen for why the week changed and what Nadia will do next. Then retell the exchange using one past event, one future plan, one comparison and one polite request.

Move a study meeting
Nadia and Sam arrange a new time for their French study group.
NadiaLa réunion est jeudi, pas mardi. Mardi, je travaille jusqu'à 17 h.The meeting has been rescheduled. On Tuesday, I work until 5 p.m.
SamJe comprends. Est-ce que jeudi est possible ?I understand. Is Thursday possible?
NadiaOui. La deuxième option est plus pratique que mardi.Yes. The second option is more practical than Tuesday.
SamJe vais prévenir les membres du groupe et leur donner le nouvel horaire.I am going to tell the group members and give them the new schedule.
NadiaPourriez-vous aussi confirmer le point de départ ?Could you also confirm the starting point?
SamBien sûr. Le groupe part du centre après le cours.Of course. The group leaves from the centre after class.
NadiaJe vais en parler à Marie et je vais lui envoyer un message.I will tell Marie about it and send her a message.
SamParfait. Ensuite, nous faisons un essai sans aide.Perfect. Then we will do an attempt without help.
Handle an unexpected route change
Karim describes a bus delay and confirms the new route with a classmate.
KarimIl pleuvait quand le bus est arrivé, et j'ai pris un nouvel itinéraire.It was raining when the bus arrived, and I took a new route.
NadiaTu as lu l'avis de service ?Did you read the service notice?
KarimOui. Le nouvel horaire indique un départ près du centre.Yes. The departure point is moved near the centre.
NadiaJe vais demander le point de départ à l'employée.I am going to ask the staff member for the starting point.
KarimTu lui demandes aussi l'heure ?Will you also ask her for the time?
NadiaOui. Je vais y aller après le cours et y noter l'heure.Yes. I am going there after class and note the time there.
KarimMerci. Pourriez-vous me dire si l'arrêt est près de l'entrée ?Thanks. Could you tell me whether the stop is near the entrance?
NadiaJe ne sais pas encore. Je confirme le lieu avant de partir.I do not know yet. I will confirm the place before leaving.

Follow the exchange
Choose the detail each speaker gives.
1. Why does Nadia need to move the study meeting?
A She works until 5 p.m. on TuesdayB She has a repair appointmentC The study group has no roomD Her bus arrives at 5 p.m.
2. What does Sam agree to tell the group?
A The new scheduleB The new exam resultC The library priceD The repair procedure
3. What happened while Karim was waiting for the bus?

4. What does Nadia plan to ask the staff member?

12.5 Story: a full week becomes a transfer task

Read once for the main change. On a second pass, identify the past background, the completed events, the future plan and the reason for Nadia's choice. Then retell the story with different details.

STORY

Nadia travaille et suit un cours de français; elle prépare aussi une révision finale.

12.7 Reading: scan new messages and compare details

Read once for the situation, then locate the time, place or reason. Do not translate every word; use the format and clues to find what matters.

5. What does the sender ask the reader to confirm?
A The new schedule and starting pointB The practice score and review sheetC The route price and food orderD The lesson level and study group

12.8 Writing: complete a Task 1-style practical message

WRITING 12.8W
Explain a change and make a plan
Write 60-120 French words to a classmate. Explain one schedule or route change, say what happened and what you plan to do next, give one reason for your choice, and ask for one missing detail politely. This capstone uses the official Task 1 word range, but the prompt remains original practice material.
Check your text:
  • Address the right person and make the purpose clear.
  • Connect a past event, the new plan and one reason.
  • Use one polite request and make its reference clear.
  • Keep the message within 60-120 words for this Task 1-style practice.
  • Close your notes and write a second message with new dates and a different problem.

12.9 Speaking: show what you can do without a script

SPEAKING 12.9S
Tell Marie about a changing week
Part 1: speak for up to two minutes about your real or invented week; Marie asks two follow-up questions you have not seen. Part 2: complete a short information-gap role-play about a changed appointment or route. Part 3: give a supported 30-second opinion about which option you would choose and one reason. The last two are learning previews, not a claim that you can yet complete the full official oral tasks.
  • Start with the main idea and add the reason or detail the listener needs.
  • When Marie asks an unseen follow-up, answer it directly before adding extra information.
  • Use a repair phrase if you lose a word: Je veux dire…; Pourriez-vous répéter ?.
  • On the retry, change the situation and answer without copying the model.

12.10 TCF corner and checkpoint

This is an A2 learning capstone, not a TCF mock exam. Its original listening and reading questions practise common task formats. The writing block uses the official TCF Canada Task 1 range of 60-120 words. Tasks 2 and 3, and speaking Tasks 2 and 3, are only guided previews here; their full demands require later level work. Official current task lengths and timing are listed in the reviewer notes, not inferred from this lesson.

LISTENING 12.10L
TCF listening practice
Listen once; choose the answer supported by the recording.
1. When is the new appointment?
A Thursday at 2 p.m.B Tuesday at 2 p.m.C Thursday at 4 p.m.D Friday at 2 p.m.
2. What is true about the second option?
A It is longer but simplerB It is shorter but less clearC It is cancelledD It starts earlier
3. What does the speaker want repeated?
A The starting pointB The meeting timeC The reason for the delayD The review target
READING 12.10R
TCF reading practice
Read each short message or notice once for the requested information.
HORAIRE - Cours : 18 h. Réunion déplacée : jeudi à 19 h. Point de départ : entrée principale.
1. Where is the meeting point?
A At the main entranceB In the classroomC At the station platformD Near the bus stop
NOTE PERSONNELLE - Je choisis l'option la plus simple. Elle est plus longue, mais le lieu est près du centre.
2. Why did the writer choose this option?
A It is simpler and near the centreB It is shorter and freeC It has a different meeting timeD It avoids all travel
RAPPEL - Après le test blanc, une erreur fréquente est dans vos notes. Choisissez un point à revoir demain.
3. What should the learner do after the practice test?
A Note an error and choose a point to reviewB Write a long opinion articleC Cancel the next classD Repeat the same test immediately
READING 12.10C
Checkpoint: 10 questions
Aim for 8/10. For each missed item, name the clue and retry after a delay.
1. Choose the sentence that connects a background and a completed event.
A Il pleuvait quand le bus est arrivé.B Il a pleuvoir quand le bus arrivait demain.C Il pleut quand le bus est arrivé demain.D Il pleuvra quand le bus arrivait.
2. Choose the correct near-future plan.
A Je vais demander l'heure.B Je demander vais l'heure.C Je vais demandé l'heure.D Je l'heure vais demander.
3. Choose the correct comparison.
A La deuxième option est plus simple que la première.B La deuxième option plus simple la première.C La deuxième option est que plus simple la première.D La deuxième option est plus simple à la première.
4. Choose the correct direct object pronoun for le message: Je ___ lis.
A leB laC luiD y
5. Choose the correct indirect pronoun for à Nadia: Je ___ demande l'heure.
A luiB laC enD y
6. Replace au centre in Je vais au centre; je ___ vais après le cours.
A yB luiC leurD en
7. Replace des pommes and keep the amount: J'___ prends quatre.
A enB yC lesD leur
8. Choose the correct polite request.
A Pourriez-vous confirmer l'horaire ?B Pourriez-vous confirmez l'horaire ?C Confirme l'horaire !D Vous confirmer l'horaire ?
9. Choose the correct negative instruction.
A Ne changez pas le point de départ.B Ne changez le point de départ pas.C Ne changez-vous pas le point de départ.D Ne pas changez le point de départ.
10. Which response gives the clearest next step after a schedule changes?
A Je vais confirmer l'heure et prévenir le groupe.B Oui, la semaine est très longue.C Je n'aime pas les messages.D C'est à gauche, mais je ne sais pas.

A2 Unit 12: retrieve, then transfer

  • After one day, close your notes and retrieve five useful words and the grammar pattern.
  • After three and seven days, use the same language in a changed scene; bring back items that still need effort.
  • Record one self-correction and one original sentence in your notes, then say the sentence without reading.
  • Move on when you can do the transfer task with the notes closed; lesson completion alone is not mastery.
ANSWER KEY
Answers, audio scripts and model texts

Do the exercise first. Then check. Read the audio scripts only after you answered the listening questions.

A2 · Unit 1: A week in my life: connect the details

Reading 1.3: Choose the connector
1. B et. Et adds the second activity.
2. A mais. Mais shows the contrast between busy Monday and free Tuesday.
3. C parce que. Parce que introduces the reason: je travaille.
4. D donc. Donc introduces the result.
Reading 1.4: Follow the schedules
1. A She has a French workshop.. Nadia says she has a French workshop on Tuesday.
2. B Wednesday. She says she will review on Wednesday because she is free.
3. A At 5 p.m.. Julie says she finishes at seventeen hundred, or 5 p.m.
4. C Review French. Sam says: D'abord, on révise le français.
Reading 1.5: Understand the story
1. A Every Sunday. The first sentence says Chaque dimanche.
2. B An unexpected event. The story says un imprévu change son horaire.
3. C Wednesday. She postpones the session until Wednesday.
4. A She sees progress.. The story says she rereads her notes and sees progress.
Listening 1.6: Listen to a weekly update
Audio: D'habitude, je travaille le lundi et le mardi. Cette semaine, je suis libre mardi parce que je vais à un atelier. 1. A The speaker is attending a workshop.. The speaker says je suis libre mardi parce que je vais à un atelier.
Audio: Je prépare ma liste de tâches le dimanche. Ensuite, je vérifie les heures de mon cours et de mon travail. 2. A Checks class and work times. The next action is checking the hours for class and work.
Audio: Je suis un peu débordée, donc je reporte la séance à jeudi. Je vais me reposer ce soir. 3. C Thursday. The speaker says je reporte la séance à jeudi.
Audio: Si j'ai bien compris, tu es libre après seize heures ? Oui, c'est ça. 4. B After 16:00. The response confirms that the person is free after seize heures, or 16:00.
Audio: Je révise rarement le matin. En général, je prends des notes après le cours et je relis le tout le soir. 5. C In the evening. The speaker says je relis le tout le soir.
Reading 1.7: Read weekly messages
1. C Thursday after 18 h. Sam says he is free Thursday after 18 h.
2. B Their notes. The notice says Apporter ses notes.
3. A She is working.. Nadia writes parce que je travaille.
4. C Take a break. Pour finir introduces the last item: prendre une pause.
5. B The room. Awa asks Peux-tu préciser la salle ?
Writing 1.8W: Write to a classmate (model)
Salut Julie,

D'habitude, je travaille du lundi au jeudi et j'étudie le français le soir. Chaque dimanche, j'organise ma semaine et je prépare une liste de tâches. Cette semaine, j'ai une présentation vendredi, donc je suis un peu débordé. Je reporte ma séance de français à mercredi parce que je suis libre après dix-huit heures. Est-ce que tu peux étudier avec moi jeudi ? Nous pouvons réviser le français et prendre une pause.

À bientôt,
Sam
Speaking 1.9S: Answer follow-up questions (model)
En général, je travaille quatre jours par semaine et j'étudie le français le soir. D'habitude, je révise après le souper, mais le mercredi je suis souvent fatigué. Je prends une pause parce que je veux être en forme. Mon objectif est de parler un peu chaque jour. Cette semaine, j'ai une journée chargée, donc je vais étudier jeudi. Le dimanche, j'organise ma semaine et je prépare mes notes.
Listening 1.10L: TCF listening practice
Audio: Je travaille le matin, mais je suis libre après quinze heures. Je peux réviser avec toi jeudi. 1. A Thursday after 15:00. The speaker is free after 15:00 and says Thursday works.
Audio: D'abord, je vérifie l'échéance. Ensuite, je termine la présentation. Je prends une pause après. 2. B Finishes the presentation. Ensuite introduces the second action: finish the presentation.
Audio: Je reporte le cours parce que j'ai un imprévu au travail. 3. A The speaker has an unexpected work issue.. The reason is un imprévu au travail.
Reading 1.10R: TCF reading practice
1. C Thursday at 18 h. Awa says she can go to the session Thursday at 18 h.
2. B Write an example from memory. The notice says to write an example without looking at notes.
3. C The room. The message gives the new room: 204.
Reading 1.10C: Checkpoint: 10 questions
1. A A habit. Une habitude means a habit.
2. B en général. En général means generally.
3. C A deadline. Une échéance is a deadline.
4. C parce que. Parce que introduces the reason.
5. A donc. Donc introduces the result.
6. B To rest. Se reposer means to rest.
7. A An unexpected event. Un imprévu is an unexpected event.
8. B To clarify or specify. Préciser means to specify or clarify.
9. C D'abord, ensuite, enfin. Use d'abord, then ensuite, and finally enfin.
10. A Je reporte le cours parce que j'ai un imprévu au travail.. Parce que introduces the reason: an unexpected work problem. The other sentences either reverse the cause and result, contrast two ideas, or give a different reason.

A2 · Unit 2: What happened this week?

Reading 2.3: Build the completed action
1. A avons réservé. Use present avoir plus the past participle réservé: nous avons réservé.
2. A a terminé. With elle, use a plus terminé.
3. A avez confirmé. The order is subject + avoir + past participle: vous avez confirmé.
4. A Ils ont étudié hier.. Ont étudié is a completed past action; the other sentences are present or future.
Reading 2.4: Follow the event details
1. A By the door. Sam says he found it near the door.
2. A A refund. Awa says she asked for a refund.
3. A He prepared his presentation.. Sam says he borrowed a book and prepared his presentation.
4. A She walked.. Nadia says she walked to the station.
Reading 2.5: Understand the story
1. A An assignment. The second sentence says she finished an assignment before class.
2. A A book. She borrowed a book from the library.
3. A To report the delay. The story says she called the service to report the delivery delay.
4. A Wednesday. The service confirmed the delivery for Wednesday.
Listening 2.6: Listen to short event updates
Audio: Hier soir, j'ai terminé mon devoir et j'ai préparé mon exposé. 1. A A presentation. The speaker says j'ai préparé mon exposé.
Audio: La semaine dernière, nous avons réservé une visite guidée pour samedi. 2. A Last week. The first words are la semaine dernière.
Audio: J'ai préparé le devoir pendant une heure parce que le cours a commencé plus tard. 3. A The class started later.. The speaker says the class started later.
Audio: Avant-hier, j'ai emprunté un dictionnaire. Hier matin, j'ai terminé le devoir. 4. A The assignment. The second sentence says the speaker finished the assignment yesterday morning.
Audio: Heureusement, nous avons trouvé le bon bureau au bon moment. 5. A Relieved. Heureusement signals that finding the office was fortunate.
Reading 2.7: Read everyday event messages
1. A At 10 a.m.. Nadia writes that the visit starts at 10 h.
2. A Thursday after 9 a.m.. The notice says the service is open Thursday at 9 h.
3. A The reservation. The message says the service confirmed the reservation.
4. A A guided tour. The 13 h line says la visite guidée.
5. A He walked.. Sam says j'ai marché jusqu'à la station.

Reading 2.10R: TCF reading practice
1. A Thursday at 10 a.m.. The notice places the presentation on Thursday at 10 h.
2. A The complaint. The message says the service confirmed the complaint.

Reading 2.10C: Checkpoint: 10 questions
1. A The day before yesterday. Avant-hier means the day before yesterday.
2. A réservé. For a regular -er verb, remove -er and add -é.
3. A avons terminé. Use avons + the past participle terminé.
4. A un remboursement. Un remboursement means a refund.
5. A à cause. À cause de introduces a reason with a negative or neutral result.
6. A A fortunate result. Heureusement means fortunately.
7. A J'ai réservé une place.. The correct pattern is avoir + past participle.
8. A A present action or habit. Je réserve is present; j'ai réservé describes a completed action.
9. A The person called.. Puis means then, so the call came before the refund request.
10. A Hier, j'ai emprunté un livre.. The first sentence says the speaker borrowed a book yesterday.

A2 · Unit 3: Everyday events: what I did

Reading 3.3A: Use the pattern
1. A ai lu. For lire, the past participle is lu: j'ai lu.
2. A avons pris. Use avoir with the irregular participle pris.
3. A a vu. Voir becomes vu: elle a vu.
4. A as fait. The irregular past participle of faire is fait.
Reading 3.3B: Use the pattern
1. A est arrivée. Arriver uses être here, and the participle agrees with Lina: elle est arrivée.
2. A parties. The subject is feminine plural, so the written participle is parties.
3. A Sam est arrivé à l'heure.. Use être with arriver in this completed action, then use the past participle arrivé.
Reading 3.4: Follow the exchange
1. A One near the train station. Sam says the connection was near the train station.
2. A A city map. He says he read the city map and saw the room's name.
3. A She read an article and saw a French session.. Awa says she read an article and saw a French session.
4. A The receipt. Awa says she kept the receipt.
Reading 3.5: Understand the story
1. A A notice with the new route. The story says he saw a notice with the new route.
2. A He made a stop.. The story says he made a stop at the public library.
3. A An instruction. The story says he read a consigne, an instruction.
4. A A message about the next French session. The last sentence mentions a message about the next French session.
Listening 3.6: Listen for meaning

Reading 3.10C: Checkpoint: 10 questions
1. A fait. The irregular participle of faire is fait.
2. A avons pris. Use avoir plus the participle pris.
3. A A receipt. Un reçu is a receipt.
4. A pour la première fois. Pour la première fois means for the first time.
5. A J'ai lu le message.. The passé composé uses avoir plus the participle lu.
6. A A connection or transfer. In travel, a correspondance is a connection or transfer.
7. A The speaker took the bus.. Ensuite means next, so taking the bus happened second.
8. A I got an answer.. Avoir has the irregular participle eu: j'ai eu une réponse.
9. A At the date or time line. Scan the date or time line for the relevant schedule detail.
10. A Recount a different trip without notes. A changed trip checks whether you can retrieve and use the language independently.

A2 · Unit 4: Tell a clear story

Reading 4.3A: Use the pattern
1. A habitions. A repeated past living situation uses the imparfait: nous habitions.
2. A faisait. A repeated past weather condition uses il faisait.
3. A avait. The imparfait form is elle avait.
4. A avait. A past description uses avait.
Reading 4.3B: Use the pattern
1. A préparions. The sentence describes a repeated routine, so use the imparfait: préparions.
2. A avons préparé. This reports one completed event: avons préparé.
3. A La salle était calme quand le cours a commencé.. Était describes the scene; a commencé reports the completed event.
4. A A repeated past habit. Chaque matin and the imparfait describe a repeated past habit.
Reading 4.4: Follow the exchange
1. A She took the bus each morning.. Awa says she used to take the bus every morning.
2. A A small café. Awa remembers a small café near the stop.
3. A In the basement. Nadia says it was in the basement.
4. A The light near the window was soft.. She says the light was soft near the window.
Reading 4.5: Understand the story
1. A Near the college. The first sentence says she lived near the college.
2. A She took the bus at seven.. The second sentence gives her repeated morning routine.
3. A On the second floor. The story places the room on the second floor.
4. A At her aunt's home. The final sentence says they met at her aunt's home.
5. A They prepared the meal together.. The final sentence reports one completed event: the family prepared a meal together.
Listening 4.6: Listen for meaning
Audio: À cette époque, le centre était paisible le matin et la rue était calme. 1. A Quiet. The speaker describes it as paisible, quiet.
Audio: Chaque mardi, nous prenions le bus après le travail et nous rentrions à dix-huit heures. 2. A Every Tuesday after work. The recording says chaque mardi and après le travail.
Audio: Il y avait une cour intérieure avec un banc près de l'entrée. 3. A A bench. The recording mentions a bench near the entrance.
Audio: Pendant l'hiver, le ciel était souvent couvert, mais la pièce restait calme. 4. A The sky. The speaker says the sky was often overcast.
Audio: Le dimanche, ma tante préparait le repas pendant que nous parlions dans la cuisine. 5. A The meal. The recording says the aunt prepared the meal.

2. A Two. The note says deux fenêtres.

2. A A pedestrian crossing. The second sentence mentions a pedestrian crossing near the park.
3. A At 6 p.m.. The message says à 18 h.
Reading 4.10C: Checkpoint: 10 questions
1. A -ions. With nous, the imparfait ending is -ions: nous parlions.
2. A était. The imparfait form is elle était.
3. A A shower of rain. Une averse is a shower of rain.
4. A Chaque matin. Chaque matin signals repetition.
5. A habitions. A past routine uses nous habitions.
6. A La salle était calme.. Était calme describes a past state.
7. A Take parlons, remove -ons. Use the present nous form parlons, remove -ons, then add the ending.
8. A ét-. Être uses the irregular stem ét- in the imparfait.
9. A un immeuble ancien. Un immeuble ancien means an older building.
10. A Describe a different past routine with notes closed. A new routine with notes closed checks independent retrieval and transfer.

A2 · Unit 5: Organise work, study and future plans

Reading 5.3A: Use the pattern
1. A allons préparer. Use present allons plus the infinitive préparer.
2. A va participer. With elle, present aller is va, followed by the infinitive participer.
Reading 5.3B: Use the pattern
1. A préparerai. With je, préparer takes the futur simple ending -ai: je préparerai.
2. A je serai. The irregular stem is ser- and the je ending is -ai: je serai.
3. A Si le cours est complet, je choisirai un autre horaire.. Use present after si for this condition, then futur simple in the result: je choisirai.
4. A Nous répondrons demain.. Drop final e from répondre, then add -ons: nous répondrons.
Reading 5.4: Follow the exchange
1. A Tuesday at 6 p.m.. The adviser says the group starts Tuesday at eighteen hours.
2. A Check her work schedule. Mina says she will check her schedule, then confirm.
3. A He has a day shift at the scheduled time.. Sam says he has a day shift Thursday and is free Friday morning.
4. A Ask for an appointment next week. He says he will ask for an appointment the following week.
Reading 5.5: Understand the story
1. A Tuesday and Thursday evenings. The story gives Tuesdays and Thursdays from six to eight p.m.
2. A November 15. The fifth sentence says the deadline is November fifteenth.
3. A Choose another evening course. The condition and result are in the sixth sentence.
4. A An email. The adviser will send Nadia a courriel, an email.
Listening 5.6: Listen for meaning
Audio: La formation du soir commence le 4 novembre. La date limite pour envoyer le formulaire est le 28 octobre. 1. A October 28. The speaker gives October 28 as the deadline.
Audio: Je vais participer à l'entretien vendredi matin. Si l'heure change, la conseillère va m'envoyer un courriel. 2. A Send an email. The speaker says the adviser will email if the time changes.
Audio: Nous choisirons le groupe après l'entretien. La formation va commencer en janvier. 3. A In January. The recording says the course will start in January.
Audio: Je ne vais pas suivre le cours intensif cet été. Je vais m'inscrire à la formation du soir en septembre. 4. A The evening course in September. The speaker plans to enrol in the evening course in September.
Audio: Votre candidature restera en attente jusqu'à lundi. Nous allons confirmer la date de l'entretien par courriel. 5. A By email. The notice says the date will be confirmed by email.
Reading 5.7: Read everyday texts
1. A October 28. The notice gives October 28 as the deadline.
2. A No decision is given yet.. The file is received and the application is pending; no decision is stated.
3. A Starting December 2. The notice says registration starts on December 2.

Listening 5.10L: TCF listening practice

Reading 5.10R: TCF reading practice
1. A May 8. The notice says the centre will confirm places on May 8.
2. A Reply before Wednesday. The message asks the reader to reply before Wednesday.
3. A A cover letter and a transcript. The notice names a letter of motivation and a transcript.
Reading 5.10C: Checkpoint: 10 questions
1. A Nous allons vérifier. Present aller plus an infinitive: allons vérifier.
2. A serai. Être has the future stem ser- and the je ending -ai.
3. A soumettra. Use the infinitive stem with the elle ending -a.
4. A tu auras. The irregular future stem is aur-; with tu, add -as.
5. A Si le groupe est complet, je choisirai un autre horaire.. Use present after si, then the future in the result.
6. A The course will start in January.. Commencera is the future simple form of commencer.
7. A nous répondrons. Drop the final e from répondre, then add -ons: répondrons.
8. A Je vais demander une autre date.. Je vais plus the infinitive demander expresses an intention.
9. A The schedule is not confirmed yet.. Pending confirmation means the schedule has not been confirmed.
10. A Nous irons au centre lundi.. The future stem is ir-; nous takes -ons: irons.

A2 · Unit 6: Choose a home and describe a neighbourhood

Reading 6.3A: Use the pattern
1. A moins. Use moins + adjective + que to say it costs less.
2. A Le logement B a plus de rangement que le studio.. The pattern is plus de + noun + que.
3. A C'est la pièce la plus lumineuse.. Use la for feminine pièce and lumineuse agrees with it.
4. A meilleur. Use masculine meilleur before the masculine noun accès.
Reading 6.4: Follow the exchange
1. A The studio. Karim says the studio costs $1,350, while the one-bedroom costs $1,450.
2. A A peaceful neighbourhood and a short trip. Lina says she prefers a peaceful neighbourhood and a short trip.
3. A In a shared room in the basement. The contact says they are in a shared room in the basement.
4. A Indoor parking. The contact says indoor parking costs extra each month.
Reading 6.5: Understand the story
1. A A library and a bus stop. The first sentence names a library and a bus stop.
2. A A washer in the home. Listing B has a washer; listing A has a building laundry room but no washer in the studio.
3. A It has lower rent and suits her priorities.. She wants a short trip and affordable rent and does not need a balcony.
4. A The electricity costs. The last two sentences say she will check electricity costs after visiting.
Listening 6.6: Listen for meaning

Listening 6.10L: TCF listening practice

Reading 6.10R: TCF reading practice
1. A Outdoor parking. The notice says outdoor parking is included.
2. A The home is five minutes from the train.. The notice places the apartment five minutes from the train.
3. A Confirm the visit time by email. The notice asks the reader to confirm the visit time by email.
Reading 6.10C: Checkpoint: 10 questions
1. A moins. Moins + adjective + que compares two prices.
2. A lumineux. The subject logement is masculine singular, so use lumineux.
3. A moins de bruit. Use moins de + noun for a smaller quantity.
4. A la pièce la plus grande. The article and adjective agree with feminine pièce.
5. A better access. Meilleur is the irregular comparative of bon before a noun.
6. A Le logement A est moins cher que le logement B.. A costs less than B, so use moins cher que.
7. A The monthly total will be higher than rent alone.. An extra electricity cost is added to the stated rent.
8. A C'est le quartier le plus paisible.. Use le with masculine quartier and place it before plus paisible.
9. A Are electricity costs included in the rent?. Ask for the missing cost detail before comparing monthly totals.
10. A Le logement A coûte moins cher et l'arrêt est plus près.. That option supports both stated needs: lower rent and a closer stop.

A2 · Unit 7: Health appointments and getting help

Reading 7.3A: Use the pattern
1. A que. The clinic asks for the form; the form receives the action, so use que.
2. A qui. The professional explains; the missing noun is the subject, so use qui.
3. A où. The missing information is a place, so use où.
4. A qu'. The note receives the action of sending; before elle, que becomes qu'.
Reading 7.4: Follow the exchange
1. A Two days. Awa says depuis deux jours.

Reading 7.10C: Checkpoint: 10 questions
1. A qui. The professional does the action of answering, so use qui.
2. A que. The service sends the message; the message receives the action, so use que.
3. A où. Use où for the place of the appointment.
4. A qu'. Que becomes qu' before a vowel sound.
5. A Le document que je prépare. The document receives the action of preparing, so use que.
6. A La clinique où j'ai rendez-vous. Use où for the place where the appointment happens.
7. A le moment où le symptôme apparaît. Où can refer to a time as well as a place.
8. A Le service qui suit mon dossier a appelé.. The service is the subject of suit, so use qui.
9. A When it began and when it happens. The unit practises reporting neutral timing and observable details.
10. A In the confirmation message. Follow the notice's stated source for the address.

A2 · Unit 8: Ask, answer and follow up at work or school

Reading 8.3A: Use the pattern
1. A le. Compte rendu is masculine singular, so replace it with le.
2. A la. Version is feminine singular, so use la.
3. A les. Fichiers is plural, and the pronoun goes before the infinitive classer.
4. A l'. Plan is masculine singular, so use le; it shortens to l' before organiser.

Reading 8.10C: Checkpoint: 10 questions
1. A le. Rapport is masculine singular, so use le.
2. A l'. Note is feminine singular, so use la; before envoyer it becomes l'.
3. A les. Dossiers is plural, so use les before the infinitive.
4. A Before the conjugated verb: Je la corrige. A direct object pronoun goes before its conjugated verb.
5. A Je vais les envoyer.. With aller + infinitive, put the pronoun before the infinitive: vais les envoyer.
6. A Je ne le trouve pas.. In a simple negative, ne and pas go around the conjugated verb, with the pronoun before it.
7. A Le dossier. L' can replace one masculine or feminine singular object before a vowel; dossier is singular and masculine.
8. A Several documents or files. Les replaces a plural direct object.
9. A Sam. Sam is the subject; le is the report being reread.
10. A Tu parles de la liste de corrections ?. Name the possible noun and check the reference before acting.

3. A Je vais en acheter un litre.. Put en before the infinitive and keep the amount after it.
4. A Je n'en prends pas.. In this pattern, ne comes before en and pas follows the verb.
5. A The number two. The number remains after the verb and tells the amount.
6. A The lentils. En avoids repeating the food phrase already named.
7. A Oui, j'en veux.. Use en to replace du lait végétal.
8. A J'en prends une barquette.. The package amount une barquette follows the verb.
9. A Je vais en acheter un kilo.. Put en before the infinitive acheter.
10. A J'en prends deux barquettes.. Keep the quantity after the verb and name the package if useful.

A2 · Unit 11: Solve a practical problem politely

Reading 11.3A: Use the pattern
1. A Cochez la case obligatoire.. Use the vous imperative for an unfamiliar service contact.
2. A N'appuyez pas sur le bouton.. Before a vowel sound, ne shortens to n'; pas follows the verb.
3. A The final -s is removed: coche.. The familiar imperative of a regular -er verb drops the final -s.
Reading 11.3B: Use the pattern
1. A Pourriez-vous vérifier le numéro de suivi ?. Use conditional pourriez-vous followed by the infinitive vérifier.
2. A Je voudrais une réponse écrite.. Use je voudrais followed by the noun phrase.
3. A Pourriez-vous m'expliquer la prochaine étape ?. Pourriez-vous + infinitive is a courteous request in this scenario.
Reading 11.4: Follow the exchange
1. A The delivery address. The employee asks him to confirm the delivery address.
2. A A new delivery. He says he would like a new delivery.
3. A A photo. The contact asks her to attach a photo to the request.
4. A A written reply. She asks for a written reply.
Reading 11.5: Understand the story
1. A The form. The first sentence says Sam receives an incomplete form.
2. A A signature. The second sentence says a signature is missing.
3. A A confirmation number. The page of confirmation gives a confirmation number.
4. A Two days. The employee tells Sam that the processing time is two days.
Listening 11.6: Listen for meaning
Audio: Le formulaire est incomplet. Il manque une signature. Cochez la case obligatoire et appuyez sur le bouton de validation. 1. A A signature. The recording says a signature is missing.
Audio: Je n'ai pas reçu le colis. Pourriez-vous vérifier le numéro de suivi ? 2. A The tracking number. The request is to check the tracking number.
Audio: La fuite d'eau est près du robinet. Je voudrais un rendez-vous d'entretien cette semaine. 3. A A maintenance appointment. The speaker asks for a maintenance appointment this week.
Audio: Pourriez-vous m'envoyer une réponse écrite ? Je confirme le délai de traitement demain. 4. A Tomorrow. The recording says the processing time will be confirmed tomorrow.
Audio: Le service à la clientèle va traiter la demande. La page de confirmation donne le numéro. 5. A On the confirmation page. The recording says the confirmation page gives the number.
Reading 11.7: Read everyday texts
1. A A double-sided copy and proof of address. The form message names a copy and proof of address.
2. A Thursday from 2 to 4 p.m.. The notice says the next time slot is Thursday from 14 h to 16 h.
3. A The tracking number. The message labels 4071 as the tracking number.
4. A A written reply. The service notice says it will provide a written reply.
5. A Thursday at 10 a.m.. The notice gives Thursday at 10 h.
Writing 11.8W: Explain the problem and ask for a next step (model)
Bonjour,

Je n'ai pas reçu le colis. Voici le numéro de suivi : 4071. Pourriez-vous vérifier la confirmation de livraison et me dire quel est le prochain créneau ? Je voudrais une réponse écrite, s'il vous plaît. Je peux prendre le colis jeudi après 14 h. Merci de confirmer le délai de traitement et la prochaine étape.

Merci,
Nadia
Speaking 11.9S: Explain a service problem to Marie (model)
Bonjour. Il y a une fuite d'eau près du robinet. Pourriez-vous confirmer le rendez-vous d'entretien ? Je voudrais une réponse écrite avec le délai de traitement. Dois-je attacher une photo à ma demande ? Merci. Je confirme : vous m'envoyez la date demain ?
Listening 11.10L: TCF listening practice
Audio: Je n'ai pas le colis. Pourriez-vous vérifier le numéro de suivi et confirmer le prochain créneau ? 1. A Check the tracking number and confirm the next time slot. The speaker asks to check the number and confirm the time slot.
Audio: Il manque un justificatif de domicile. Attachez la copie recto verso avant d'appuyer sur le bouton. 2. A The double-sided copy. The message says to attach the double-sided copy.
Audio: Je voudrais une réponse écrite. Le service va traiter la demande demain. 3. A Tomorrow. The message says the service will handle it tomorrow.
Reading 11.10R: TCF reading practice
1. A Friday from 9 to 11 a.m.. The confirmation gives Friday from 9 h to 11 h.
2. A After adding the copy. The notice says not to press the button before adding the copy.
3. A The confirmation number. The notice asks the reader to keep the confirmation number.
Reading 11.10C: Checkpoint: 10 questions
1. A Pourriez-vous vérifier le numéro de suivi ?. Use pourriez-vous followed by the infinitive vérifier.
2. A N'appuyez pas sur le bouton.. Use n' before a vowel sound, with pas after the verb.
3. A Coche la case.. A tu imperative of regular -er cocher drops final -s.
4. A Je voudrais une réponse écrite.. Je voudrais is followed by the noun phrase une réponse écrite.
5. A Pourriez-vous envoyer la confirmation ?. Use the infinitive envoyer after pourriez-vous.
6. A Pourriez-vous vérifier le formulaire ?. A polite conditional request with vous fits an unfamiliar service interaction.
7. A Ne signez pas le formulaire incomplet.. Use the vous imperative without a subject; place ne...pas around the verb.
8. A Je voudrais une réponse écrite, s'il vous plaît.. This is clear, specific and politely phrased.
9. A A specific action request and a next-step question. State what happened, ask for one useful action, and confirm the next step.
10. A Merci, je confirme : vous envoyez la réponse demain ?. Restating the action and checking the timing confirms the next step respectfully.

A2 · Unit 12: A2 capstone: handle a changing week

Reading 12.3A: Use the pattern
1. A a lu. A finished action uses a lu, the passé composé form of lire.
2. A Il pleuvait quand le bus est arrivé.. The ongoing weather is background (pleuvait); the arrival is a completed event (est arrivé).
3. A Je vais appeler le centre.. Use the present form of aller followed by the infinitive.
4. A le. Document is masculine singular; use direct object pronoun le.
5. A lui. Lui replaces one person after demander à.
6. A y. Y replaces the named place.
7. A en. Use en and keep the quantity quatre.
8. A Pourriez-vous confirmer l'heure ?. The conditional pourriez-vous makes this a polite request.
Reading 12.4: Follow the exchange
1. A She works until 5 p.m. on Tuesday. Nadia says she works until 17 h on Tuesday.
2. A The new schedule. He says he will tell the group the new schedule.
3. A It was raining when the bus arrived. Karim says it was raining when the bus arrived.
4. A The address and time. Nadia says she will ask for the address, and Karim asks whether she will ask for the time too.
Reading 12.5: Understand the story
1. A The meeting and Nadia's schedule. The message says the meeting is moved and her schedule changes.
2. A It was raining. The story says it was raining when the bus arrived.
3. A It is simpler, even though it is longer. The story says the option is longer but simpler.
4. A Speaking without help. She says she still needs to speak without help.
Listening 12.6: Listen for meaning
Audio: Nouvel horaire : la réunion est jeudi. Le groupe est au centre après le cours. 1. A Thursday after class. The meeting is moved to Thursday; the group meets after class.
Audio: Il pleuvait quand le bus est arrivé. J'ai pris le nouvel itinéraire et j'ai lu l'avis. 2. A Took a new route and read the notice. The recording says the speaker took a new route and read the notice.
Audio: La deuxième option est plus simple, mais elle est plus longue. Je vais y aller après le cours. 3. A It is simpler but longer. The speaker says it is simpler but longer.
Audio: Pourriez-vous confirmer le point de départ ? Je voudrais le noter avant de partir. 4. A The starting point. The request is to confirm the starting point.
Audio: Après le test blanc, j'ai une erreur fréquente dans mes notes. Je vais la revoir demain. 5. A Tomorrow. The speaker says they will review it tomorrow.
Reading 12.7: Read everyday texts
1. A Thursday at 6 p.m.. The message says Thursday at 18 h.
2. A Thursday at 2 p.m.. The schedule gives the appointment on Thursday at 14 h.
3. A Its starting point is closer to the centre. The note says its starting point is closer, although it is longer.
4. A Speaking for 45 seconds. The sheet gives a 45-second speaking goal without help.
5. A The new schedule and starting point. The sender asks for both the schedule and starting point.
Writing 12.8W: Explain a change and make a plan (model)
Bonjour Sam,

La réunion est jeudi à 18 h, pas mardi. Mardi, je travaille jusqu'à 17 h. Il pleuvait quand le bus est arrivé hier, et j'ai lu l'avis puis pris un nouvel itinéraire. La deuxième option est plus longue, mais le point de départ est plus près du centre. Je vais y aller après mon cours. Pourriez-vous confirmer l'entrée principale ? Je vais prévenir les membres du groupe et leur envoyer l'horaire provisoire.

Merci,
Nadia
Speaking 12.9S: Tell Marie about a changing week (model)
Cette semaine, je travaille et je suis un cours de français. Mardi, un imprévu a changé mon horaire. Il pleuvait quand le bus est arrivé, et j'ai pris un nouvel itinéraire. Je vais demander l'heure à l'employée et confirmer le point de départ. Je préfère la deuxième option parce qu'elle est plus simple, mais elle est plus longue. Je voudrais aussi prévenir mon groupe.
Listening 12.10L: TCF listening practice
Audio: Le rendez-vous est jeudi à 14 h. Je vais confirmer le nouvel horaire ce soir. 1. A Thursday at 2 p.m.. The appointment is moved to Thursday at 14 h.
Audio: Il pleuvait quand le bus est arrivé. La deuxième option est plus longue, mais plus simple. 2. A It is longer but simpler. The recording directly compares the two qualities.
Audio: Pourriez-vous répéter le point de départ ? Je voudrais le noter. 3. A The starting point. The speaker asks to hear the starting point again.
Reading 12.10R: TCF reading practice
1. A At the main entrance. The schedule names the main entrance as the starting point.
2. A It is simpler and near the centre. The note says it is the simplest option and the place is near the centre.
3. A Note an error and choose a point to review. The reminder asks for one error and one review point after the test.
Reading 12.10C: Checkpoint: 10 questions
1. A Il pleuvait quand le bus est arrivé.. The background is in the imparfait; the completed arrival is in the passé composé.
2. A Je vais demander l'heure.. Use aller plus an infinitive for the near future.
3. A La deuxième option est plus simple que la première.. Use plus + adjective + que to compare the two options.
4. A le. Message is masculine singular; use le.
5. A lui. Lui replaces one person after demander à.
6. A y. Use y to refer to the previously named place.
7. A en. Use en and keep the quantity quatre.
8. A Pourriez-vous confirmer l'horaire ?. Use pourriez-vous followed by an infinitive.
9. A Ne changez pas le point de départ.. In this negative imperative, ne...pas goes around the verb.
10. A Je vais confirmer l'heure et prévenir le groupe.. It states a concrete action and who will be informed.
